C2. Depletion ledger (switch maker.depletion_ledger: v1_2 true, v1.1 false; fill_engine.py in the core)

  • Item 1, tests. tests/test_queue_v2_ledger.py grew from 21 to 43 tests. Every fixture now also runs on the v1.1 settings (scalar queue, proportional, fill_evidence trade_confirm, ledger_epoch on, evidence window 20 ms), in both arrival orders, with 0, 10 and 30 of size behind our order. Your expectation holds exactly, switch off: book first leaves the queue ahead at 8 (10 behind) and 9 (30 behind) where print first leaves 6; with nobody behind and own size 10, head drop 10 then print 12 fills 10 instead of 2 (trade_after_front uses the whole print although 10 of it already moved the head). Both are pinned in the tests as today's behaviour.
  • Item 2, the rule. Each depth decrease records the ahead movement it applied, per order, in its netting credit. When its print arrives, the print volume that was already applied as head movement (matched volume, first come first matched, never more than the print) is not charged again: the queue ahead and the order see print − movement already applied. The part of a matched print the decrease did not apply therefore reaches the queue ahead, and then the order, exactly as if the print had come first.
  • Item 3. trade_after_front uses only the print left after that movement (the budget is the gross print minus the movement applied to that order).
  • Item 4, counters. A netting (print against an earlier decrease, or decrease against an earlier print) is counted as no other proof when the two exchange stamps are more than 1 ms apart AND the sizes differ. Tiers kept: exact (same stamp window and same size), size_or_time, no_proof. Diag keys ledger_netted_prints_no_proof, ledger_netted_decreases_no_proof and their volumes (only with the switch on) and the fill flag netting_without_proof; kept apart from ledger_ambiguous (competing credits). With the switch off the counts go to a separate ledger_stats (observation only), so v1.1 outputs are unchanged.

Reading it. With the switch on the two arrival orders agree in every fixture where the print is at least as large as the decrease (the real case, since a batch of prints shows as one decrease), for 0, 10 and 30 behind and for own size 10 and 4. Print first is unchanged by the switch in every fixture. Residual, not fixed: when the decrease is larger than its print (“print smaller” rows) the orders still differ, by less than 1.2 in these fixtures: print first, the unexplained rest of the decrease is shared over the level size the engine still shows, which is stale by the print. I did not change the print-first path (it is v1.1 behaviour); say if you want it in v1_2.

  • Item 5, report only. (a) reset_credits is reached only through on_snapshot (one caller, when ledger_epoch is on; nothing else in the replay calls it). (b) A replay feed reset can happen without a snapshot: the dead-feed rule (5 s of silence) only blocks signals; nothing resets credits, the book or the epoch, and a gap in deltas is not announced anywhere (no dataset carries sequence ids). So a credit can survive a feed gap, but only for dedupe_window_s (50 ms), which bounds the harm. The data do contain snapshots: Bybit January has about 82,000 a day, March Bybit one per 2-hour slice (the one at the start), March Binance 746,000 (its best bid/ask records are loaded as snapshots, but no Binance maker order is placed in March). (c) The cohort order.queue_ahead does lag lq.ahead after a fully netted print: when the print is fully netted, on_trade returns before the cohort tail runs, so order.queue_ahead is not refreshed from the level queue after the reclassification. In a probe (cohort, four age hypotheses, five size pairs, 0/10/30 behind: 60 cases) 18 rows show the lag and 30 show different final states between the two arrival orders. (d) test_book_first_and_trade_first_converge_without_double_depletion passes because the decrease never reaches the order: the test adds its 5 behind us with a delta stamped t = 0.5, before the order is active (t = 1.0), so that delta is skipped for the order, level_now stays 10, and 15 → 11 is not a decrease for it (queue ahead stays 10 after the decrease in both orders). Both orders then see only the print and trivially agree on 6. The test therefore does not test the ledger. I did not change it or the cohort code.
  • Item 6, before and after. One change at a time, headline cancel model (proportional) only, on the four March slices and 2022-01-13. The switch changes nothing in any of them: identical signals, orders, fills, cash P&L, markouts, trade logs and fill logs (15 of 15 compared files byte-identical between switch off and on; the switch-off March run also equals the earlier d4 cell, and the switch-off January run equals the earlier message-atomic January run).

C2b. Cancel models: your two small fixes (no change to proportional or legacy results)

  • Fix 1, feasibility in the engine. _consume_proportional now keeps the proportional and power(n) share inside max(0, C − B) ≤ C_ahead ≤ min(C, A) with the same bounds cancel_ahead() uses (new clamp_feasible() in queue_v2/cancel_models.py; behind_first and ahead_first already went through cancel_ahead()). The clamp moves a value only when it lies outside the interval by more than 1e-12, so a value inside keeps its bits. Proportional is bit-identical: over 20,000 random (A, B, C) cases the real executor gives the same queue ahead with and without the clamp for proportional (0 differ). Power(n) changes only where its old share was infeasible: 565 of 20,000 cases for n = 2 and 1,325 for n = 0.5. Your example, A = 1, B = 10, C = 10.5, power n = 2, gave 10.5 × 1 / 101 = 0.104 and now gives 0.5 (engine-level test test_power_2_with_one_ahead_and_ten_behind_..., also checked on cancel_ahead itself). A property test runs the executor over a grid for every model.
  • Fix 2, cancel_ahead(). The min_baseline / all_ahead branch is removed: cancel_ahead("min_baseline" | "all_ahead", ...) raises ValueError("engine-only rule: running-minimum baseline ..."), MODELS no longer lists it, and ENGINE_ONLY names it; it is the engine's running-minimum rule, not a function of (C, A, B) and not ahead_first. power with n ≤ 0 (or NaN) raises. The old test that looped min_baseline through the arithmetic now loops MODELS. tests/test_queue_v2_cancel_models.py: 10 → 16 tests.
  • Consequence you should know about. Six tests pinned the power(n = 1.3) golden digest and failed (C9); re-pinned at your approval. Proportional, all_ahead, none, prop_through and legacy digests matched throughout.
  • What this does not touch. The engine's all_ahead (min_baseline) path in _consume is unchanged. A report built earlier with build_report.py that called cancel_ahead("min_baseline") would now raise; that script was not run in this pass. Replays that use power(n) with n other than 1 can change; none of the headline, baseline or gate runs does.

C2c. Through-print rule, volume-limited version (maker.fill_through_prints + maker.through_print_volume_limited)

7 Nov: the rule is switched OFF in v1_2 until the Step 8 re-check (C12) is approved; this section describes the 6 Nov check.

  • What it does. A print strictly through our price (a sell below our bid) means the level we rest on was consumed: the queue ahead is cleared and the order fills, limited by the print's volume (one budget shared by every order the print went through). The full version (fills the whole order whatever the print's size) is refused when a backtester starts (ValueError in CrossVenueArbBacktester.__init__; the executor itself still accepts it, because the engine-parity tests use it).
  • Item 1: four March slices, v1.1, without and with the rule (cell a, which equals the Phase A baselines, against cell tp; same code, same data, nothing else changed; EXPLORATORY, size 1, singapore, fees ASSUMED). Three of the four slices have no signal under v1.1.

  • Result: no problem found, and no effect on money. Fills, quantities, timing, cash P&L and the 30 s markout are identical in both comparisons; the only change is the label of the fill: 3 of the 4 fills in the two comparisons (1 of 1 under v1.1, 2 of 3 under v1_2) were prints strictly through our bid and are now trade_through instead of trade. Why nothing moves: each of those orders rested on a level that was not displayed (queue ahead 0) and the print was larger than the order, so clearing the queue and the volume limit change nothing. The evidence is thin (1 and 3 fills; the rule matters when an order rests behind a real queue, which these slices rarely produce), so what I can say is that it does not hurt here, not that it is validated.
  • Item 2: turned on in the next profile version. I read “next profile version” as v1_2, the version still open; the lines are in perp_perp_maker_taker_v1_2.yaml (both true) and in the switch table; v1.1 is untouched (and byte-identical, see C9). If you meant a separate v1_3, say so and I move the two lines. Tests: 2 new in tests/test_data_checks.py (the full version is refused at startup, the limited version and off both start; v1_2 has both, v1.1 has neither). The ledger and stamp cells were made without this rule; the t1 and tp2 pair above shows it changes none of them.

C3. Manifest override and preflight override are separate

  • Built. ArbBacktestConfig.allow_manifest_gap (CLI --allow-manifest-gap) is the written reason for the data-manifest gate only. With it, the preflight still blocks a CRITICAL venue unless allow_rejected_data is also set. The result's data_quality gets manifest_override and override_scope (manifest_only or manifest_and_preflight) only when the new flag is used; when only the old flag is set it serves both, as before, and no result key changes (tested), so every v1.1 / v1 / legacy_v0 baseline is untouched. A blank reason is refused. The two runners (qv2_march_run.py, qv2_run.py) take --allow-manifest-gap and refuse --allow-rejected-data for any v1_2 profile. batch_backtest.py and queue_sensitivity.py pass the new fields through.
  • In use. The March stamp cell (C5) and the through-print v1_2 cell ran with --allow-manifest-gap only: blocking: true, override: null, override_scope: manifest_only, preflight APPROVED / HEALTHY for both venues in all four slices. The earlier January and March ledger cells, the Phase B first-pass cells and the baselines ran with the old single flag (the preflight was HEALTHY in all of them, so nothing would have blocked). Tests: 5 new in tests/test_data_checks.py.

I changed neither in this pass; the core copy stays at 2 until Phase C as you decided. No replay, accounting-shadow or baseline run read the platform copy. The only code that reads a perp_research.json in the backtester path is core_shadow.py, which reads the core copy (Path(crypto_stat_arb.__file__).parent / "config") and uses only its fee rows. Before, both venues arrived 2 ms after the exchange, so Binance was 34 ms fresher than the preset says. One side effect handled in the feed: with untrusted stamps each record would draw its own delay and split an exchange message across milliseconds (the message-atomic frame keys on one receive time), so the deltas of one exchange message now keep one receive time (one sample per message, one float step apart, as recorded stamps do). Bybit crossings counted stay 0 in all four slices. v1.1 and v1 are unchanged (default None).

C6. Trade/depth matching on TRAIN days (report only, nothing changed)

  • Data and method. TRAIN days only: March 2024 Bybit 03-01 to 03-07 (7 days), January 2022 Bybit and Binance 01-05 to 01-10 (6 days, the days the v1.1 January hedge move is trained on); 00:00 to 06:00 UTC of each day (a read-only scan, out/integration/phase_b/trade_depth_match.py, a short-lived measurement script like the probe, not used by any run). A print is one trade record (multiset de-duplication, as the replays load them). A decrease is a depth message that lowers the size of the level the print hit; level sizes are tracked as OrderBook.apply_delta does. Size-aware pairing: a print is paired with the nearest-in-time decrease at its price and side that is at least as large as the print (a batch of prints shows as one decrease); equal size is reported as the strong tier. Lag = exchange time of the decrease minus that of the print. Because busy price levels have decreases all the time, every rate also comes with a placebo: the same pairing with the decreases of that price shifted by ±7.3, ±13.1 and ±29.7 s, which is the chance rate of a window. “True pairs found” is the placebo-corrected rate relative to the best window. The job ran twice, the second time alone on the machine as you asked; the 19 result files are identical.
  • Your rough check. You measured 47 % within 50 ms and 83 % within 100 ms on 20 minutes of 03-01. My stricter size-aware pairing gives 39.0 % and 72.8 % for all seven March days (34 % and 61 % on the same 20 minutes). The shape agrees: depth arrives in 100 ms batches, about 60 ms after the print at the median. The chance rate is 3.0 % at 50 ms and 5.8 % at 100 ms.

  • Prints with no matching decrease are in the first table: March Bybit 5.1 % have no decrease at their price at all within 1 s and 2.2 % only smaller ones (7.3 % unpaired); January Bybit 1.3 % and 0.6 % (1.9 %); January Binance 0.8 % and 5.4 % (6.2 %). Such a print is a trade that left the displayed depth unchanged (hidden size, or an add at the same moment), or whose twin fell outside 1 s.
  • What a missed pair does to queue_ahead. It is counted twice. Print first (93 to 97 % of true pairs): the print drains the queue ahead by its volume; the twin decrease arrives after the credit has expired, is treated as an unexplained cancellation and drains the queue ahead again by its proportional share. Decrease first: the decrease moves the queue ahead by its share, the print then arrives with no credit and drains its full volume. Either way queue_ahead falls faster than the exchange's queue, so fills come too early: optimistic. A missed confirmation (trade_confirm, decrease first, print later than 20 ms) is the other way: the print is not accepted as evidence for the front-marked order and fills it only by the ordinary rule: pessimistic, but it concerns only 3 to 7 % of true pairs. For March the 50 ms window misses about half of the true pairs.
  • False-match rate of a longer window is the placebo share of the matches in the window table (March Bybit: 7.8 % at 50 ms, 7.9 % at 100 ms, 10.4 % at 150 ms, 12.9 % at 200 ms, 17.3 % at 300 ms; January Bybit 2.3 / 4.1 / 5.7 / 7.3 %; January Binance 9.6 / 15.6 / 20.1 / 23.8 %). A false match nets a print against an unrelated decrease: the queue advances too slowly (pessimistic).
  • Proposal (not applied; your approval first). March Bybit: dedupe_window_s 0.15 s (finds 98.8 % of true pairs, false share 10.4 %; 0.10 s finds 92.5 % at 7.9 % and is the milder alternative), evidence_window_ms 50 (covers 96 % of depth-first pairs; 20 ms covers 44 %). January Bybit: keep 50 ms and 20 ms (50 ms finds all true pairs, 20 ms covers 98 % of depth-first). January Binance: keep 50 ms (100 % of true pairs, 9.6 % false), 20 ms covers all of its depth-first pairs (few). The March value comes from a feed whose depth arrives in 100 ms batches: it is a property of that collector and must not be carried to another dataset without its own measurement, so the window belongs in the dataset block (B7), not in the strategy rules. Choosing the larger window errs towards the pessimistic side, which is the side you asked me to prefer. I will change nothing and rerun nothing until you decide.

C7. Step-3 check (report only; nothing fixed)

  • Hypothetical-order tests on the v1.1 and v1_2 settings. tests/test_queue_v2_hypothetical_order.py (8 tests) was run with its executor settings replaced by (a) the original (fill_evidence off), (b) v1.1 (trade_confirm, ledger_epoch on, 20 ms) and (c) v1_2 (v1.1 plus depletion_ledger); the three PostOnly tests, which build their own backtester, were given the same maker settings. 8 of 8 pass in all three; no failure to report.
  • Orders on the unknown-queue fallback of 25: none. Across the 166 existing v1.1 and v1_2 runs (59 January, 107 March; 972 + 27 + 82 = 1,081 order records, many of them the same configuration re-run at different gates), queue_unknown is 0, no unknown_queue_* counter was written, and no order's queue ahead equals 25 on an absent level. Every absent level was inside the captured depth (absent_level_inside_depth): 768 of 972 January orders, 27 of 27 and 31 of 82 March orders, which start with queue ahead 0 (a genuinely empty level). The 15 to 213 “queue exactly 25” rows seen in the older gate folders belong to legacy_v0 and v1, where 25 is the legacy min_queue_ahead floor, not the v1.1 fallback.
  • View book against truth book at placement. The existing logs record only the view value. I reconstructed both from the raw data for the 53 existing Bybit orders of the ledger cells and the first-pass baselines (January 01-13: 34, 01-05: 12; March: 7), a read-only scan (placement_view_vs_truth.py), not a replay. VIEW = the level size at the order's price among records received by the placement time; TRUTH = the size among records with an exchange time up to the placement time minus the host-exchange offset (median of the 10 s before: 43 ms in January, 2 ms in March because the March stamps are constructed). The reconstruction reproduces the logged level size for 51 of 53 orders (two January orders log 296.1 and 317.0 where I get 12.8 and 74.6; unexplained, so those two are uninformative). 42 of 53 orders rest on a level that is absent in both books (queue ahead 0 either way). Of the 11 on a non-empty level, 10 have the same size in both books and 1 differs: 196.5 in the view against 83.6 in the truth (view 113 larger, 57 %), January 13. March: 0 of 7 differ (by construction of the stamps). Sample: 11 non-empty-level orders, so this is an indication of size, not a measure.
  • Should placement use the truth book, as PostOnly does? Yes, in my view. PostOnly is judged on the book the exchange held at arrival (truth book, when it is complete at the arrival time); the queue the order then joins should be that same book, otherwise one arrival is judged on two different books, and the view book lags the exchange by the feed delay (the 43 ms host offset in January; with the stamp change of C5 Binance would lag 36 ms and Bybit 2 ms). In the one existing case that differs, the view put the order 113 behind a queue that the exchange no longer had. Proposal: a v1_2 switch placement_book: truth using the same availability test as PostOnly (_truth_complete_at), view book as the fallback with a counter, measured alone, one change at a time. Expected effect on the existing slices is small (10 of 11 equal); it matters for orders that rest behind real queues. Not built; waiting for your approval.

C11. Cohort queue fixes (guide step 6, 7 Nov; tests only, no replay)

  • 1. The cohort queue runs with the cross rule. _clear_ahead(order) in simulation/fill_engine.py clears the cohorts ahead (LevelQueue.clear_ahead) and sets queue_ahead to 0; with the scalar queue it only sets 0. Used at both confirmed-cross sites, the vanished-level site (two: proportional and evidence), the queue-empty threshold and the through-print path. The refusal of opposite_cross_fills with queue_model: cohort is removed (the amend refusal stays). Known limit: clear_ahead also removes another own order that rests ahead at the same price (two own orders at one level); untouched, noted.
  • 2. queue_ahead follows the cohort queue. Refresh (copy lq.ahead onto queue_ahead) after reclassify in on_trade (before the early return of a fully netted print), after the pre-arrival head move, and at the end of on_delta and on_snapshot for every live order at a level a rebase touched. Observation only: _cohort_check counts cohort_ahead_checks and cohort_ahead_mismatch_orders/events after every event (cohort mode only; nothing is written in scalar runs). expire_provisional only drops empty items and never moves the queue ahead, so it needs no refresh.
  • Why test_book_first_and_trade_first_converge passed before. It never exercised a book-first decrease. The order is placed at 1.0 and the growth to 15 is sent at 0.5, so the order never saw it; the message at 2.0 (15 to 11) is a growth of 1 against the cohort queue (10) and a non-shrink for the order (delta_shrink_events = 0). Nothing is netted and the 4-print drains the head in both orders: 6 and 6. A new test pins this. The new fixtures place the growth after activation (decrease then print, print then decrease, 0, 10 and 30 behind, three age rules): before the fix, uniform with 10 or 30 behind left queue_ahead 8 or 9 against 6 in the cohort queue after the print.
  • 3. Hazard number. cohort_hazard_theta is labelled SPOT_CALIBRATED (spot data 04-19..04-22) in the config comment and the v1_2 profile comment; a run with cohort_age: hazard raises a RuntimeWarning and every fill observation carries cohort_hazard_theta.{value, provenance}; other rules carry nothing. The range runners (run_ledger_cells.py cell co, run_b0_slices.py cell s_co) now run cohort uniform only, with the profile's cross rule, confirm window and cap-to-print; no hazard cell. The Oct-2024 pool scripts that still list a hazard cell are old and untouched.
  • Tests. tests/test_queue_v2_cohort_executor.py 11 → 23 (one old assertion changed: the cross rule is now accepted). 6 of the new tests fail on the previous engine and pass now.   • Result: the rule is inert on this probe. Same orders filled in both runs (none filled in only one), same total quantity (1,010.8), and 1,018 of the 1,020 first fills carry the identical timestamp (two are earlier with the rule, by at most 2.5 ms). Fills exactly at our price against strictly through it: with the rule off, the cause trade already covers both, because the ordinary rule treats a print below our bid as a print that reaches the order. Classified by the print that filled them, the 1,678 off-run trade fills are 486 prints exactly at our price and 1,192 prints strictly through it. With the rule on the 1,192 (plus 10 split fills) become trade_through (1,202 fills, 559.8 SOL) and 486 stay trade. On the tape, in the orders' lives: 850 prints exactly at our price (397 orders), 24,462 prints strictly through it (959 orders, 209,000 SOL). Through fills already covered by the cross rule: 57 of the 1,202 (17.5 SOL) belong to orders the off run also filled by trade_confirmed_cross; the other 1,145 were filled in the off run by the ordinary trade rule. Why it does nothing here: 2,971 of 3,213 orders rested on a level that was not displayed (queue ahead 0); the 242 that had a queue ahead and the 111 of them that saw a through print were all filled in the off run too, because a through print averages 8.5 SOL and drains the queue by itself. The five orders filled in two to four pieces (same instant, same quantity) are the volume limit working: several small prints in one burst.
  • Markout. Through fills (1,202): −0.96, −1.47, −1.74 bps at 1, 5, 30 s; fills exactly at our price: −1.42, −1.88, −2.51; cross-confirmed: −0.84, −0.72, −0.38. The through fills are not worse than the fills the model already took (they are the same orders at the same instants). There are no fills that exist only because of the rule, so there is no markout for new fills to report.
  • 2. Tests, both arrival orders (tests/test_queue_v2_invariants.py, class ThroughPrintSpentOnce, 3 pass, 4 marked OPEN as expected failures; the engine is NOT changed, the fix waits for you). The through rule is written for a print the rest of the engine treats separately, and it loses the print twice:
    1. OPEN, a netted print is dropped (arrival-order dependent). Our bid A at 100 and our bid B at 99 behind 5; a sell of 4 at 99 and the decrease 5 → 1 at 99 are one execution. Print first: A fills 3 through the rule. Book first: A fills 0, the decrease leaves a credit, the print is netted to 0 and on_trade returns before the through rule runs.
    2. OPEN, through rule and ordinary rule spend the print twice. A at 100 (through), B at 99 on an empty level (at the print's price); one sell of 4 at 99: A fills 3 (trade_through) and B fills 3 (trade): 6 from a print of 4, with or without an earlier decrease. The through budget starts at the gross print and is not reduced by the ordinary or cross fills of the same print.
    3. OPEN, through rule and cross rule spend the print twice, cross first. A at 100 is crossed and waiting, C at 99.5 is not; a sell of 4 at 99: A trade_confirmed_cross_after 3 and C trade_through 3: 6. Print first then cross: 3 + 1 = 4 (correct). The two arrival orders differ.
    4. Passing: print first through fill; a cross-pending order is filled once by the print that confirms it (cross-cap budget); print first then cross spends the print once.
    The probe cannot show these defects: it keeps one live order at a time, so no print reaches two of our orders and no depth credit exists at a level where we do not rest. I did not re-check the earlier tp and tp2 slices for them (1 to 3 fills each). They exist only when fill_through_prints is on (off in every profile now).
  • Suite after these changes: 2,716 passed, 92 failed + 1 collection error (all in tests/platform_kit, the same 93 entries as the Phase 0 list, checked by name), 15 expected failures (11 before + the 4 OPEN above). (My C11 note said 94 entries for the cohort run: that count included one guard test that my first label edit had broken; it was fixed straight away and is not in this run.)
  • 3. Proposal (not applied; waiting for your approval). The probe shows the rule is harmless and, here, does nothing; the tests show it is not yet safe where it could matter (orders behind a real queue). Proposal: keep it off until three small engine changes are made, then turn it on in v1_2, volume-limited only: (a) run the through rule before the early return of a fully netted print (gross volume, as it is written); (b) one print budget per on_trade call, reduced by every fill the print makes under any rule (cross first, then through, then ordinary), and the through budget starts from what is left; (c) a test for each, in both arrival orders (the four OPEN tests become ordinary tests). The full version stays refused: it is refused when any backtester starts (all profiles), so v1_2 cannot run it. v1.1 and legacy_v0 are untouched by all three (the rule is off there; the byte-identity gate would be re-run after the change). If you prefer, leave the rule off and drop it from the freeze list: on this evidence it changes labels, not money.
  • 4. Not modelled. Hidden liquidity ahead of us (iceberg and reserve orders that never show in the displayed size) is not modelled: our queue ahead is the displayed size only, so every queue here is a lower bound and every fill that depends on a queue clearing is, if anything, early. RPI orders (retail price improvement) on both venues are not modelled: a print that executed against an RPI order, or a quote improved by one, appears in neither our book nor our queue. Both are model gaps on the optimistic side for the maker leg; neither is measured.

  • Cohort clear_ahead. It no longer removes another own order resting ahead at the same price: the external cohorts ahead are cleared, the own order stays and is filled by the print budget or stays; queue_ahead then equals the size of that order (0 when none), a part-filled order keeps its remaining size in the queue, and a cancelled or filled order ahead moves the orders behind it up. Tests: 5 (ClearAheadKeepsOwnOrdersAhead).

  • Finding: the scalar leaves out almost all of the adverse move. The strategy prices a quote from the Binance best bid at quote time times (1 + the scalar) (arb_strategy.py, expected_hedge = hedge_px * (1 + move)), but the scalar is measured from the fill onward. For the probe fills the Binance bid moves −8.32 bps (SE 0.51) between placement and fill (mean wait 7.3 s), then −0.46 (0.08) to 45 ms after the fill: −8.78 bps from quote to hedge, of which the pin covers 5 %. This is the adverse selection: a bid 8.5 bps behind the touch fills only after the market has moved there. The gross hedge edge before fees at 45 ms is −2.2 bps (SE 0.5) against the +6.5 bps the quote showed. It affects where the strategy quotes; realised replay P&L already contains the move, because hedges execute on the book at their arrival.

The flow-sign buckets differ little. The 30 s columns have SEs of 0.5 to 2.7: do not read them.
  • 4. What block_on_toxic would have blocked. At the decision time of the 3,353 scheduled orders (Bybit state only: the probe has no Binance depth): 457 (13.6 %) would be blocked, 212 of them by the lambda regime TOXIC alone; the sign test alone is true for 1,556 orders (46 %) and the depth-collapse test alone for 615. The blocked orders produced 288 fill records (0.63 per order against 0.60 for the rest). Their markout is not worse: Binance 1 s −0.80 (0.26) against −1.07 (0.19), 30 s +0.41 (1.74) against −1.90 (0.58); hedge edge at 45 ms −2.41 (0.84) against −2.16 (0.50). Other cuts (regime TOXIC alone 124 fills, ELEVATED or TOXIC, |sign| above 0.5, depth collapse above 0.3) separate the fills no better (1 s: −0.98 to −1.15 against −0.96 to −1.10). In this probe the gate does not select adverse fills.
  • Item 4 of your earlier instruction: why 92 % of probe orders (and 79 % of January orders) rest on an empty level.
    • Distance and spread. Probe: the quote sits a median 111 ticks (8.5 bps; 10th to 90th percentile 5.8 to 11.3 bps) behind its own touch while the spread is 1 tick (0.08 bps): a price that deep is rarely a displayed level. January strategy orders (v1.1, 59 Bybit and Binance orders on 01-05 and 01-13): 88 % on an empty level; 47 % were placed inside the spread (an empty level by definition; the January spread is a median 9 ticks, 2 bps) and 77 % of the ones behind the touch were empty (median 3.3 bps behind). March v1_2 slices: 7 Bybit orders, 3 empty, 4 at the touch behind a real queue that never filled; the v1.1 March record was 27 of 27 empty.
    • How long they rest and what fills them. Probe: 32 % of orders fill, at a median 6.8 s (10th to 90th percentile 1.6 to 13.1 s; 6 % of fills inside 1 s, 38 % inside 5 s, the order lives 15 s). Of the 1,020 filled orders, 644 were filled by a print strictly through the quote (median 3 ticks through) and 376 by a print exactly at it; by rule: trade 823, cross-confirmed 157, trade after front 40. A print is about the size of the order (median 1.0 times; 2.4 times behind a real queue).
    • Markout, empty level against real queue (Binance mid, 1 / 5 / 30 s; first fill per order): empty −1.06 / −1.20 / −0.85; behind a real queue −1.00 / −2.29 / −2.98 (116 fills, SEs 0.26 / 0.65 / 1.88). Same direction; the real-queue fills are fewer and worse at 30 s but not distinguishable from the empty ones at 1 s.
  • What it means for adverse selection. An order on an empty level has no queue to protect it: it fills the moment the market trades down to or through its price, so every fill is conditional on a move that has already happened (−8.3 bps on Binance's bid before the fill), and the hedge venue and Bybit's own mid keep moving the same way afterwards. The quoting rule counts only the small post-fill piece. A real queue ahead delays the fill until volume has traded at the level and gives a better hedge edge at 45 ms (+0.8 against −2.5 bps, 189 against 1,846 fills) but a worse 30 s drift (SE 1.9): the effect is plausible and imprecise. Replay P&L is not biased by this (the hedge is simulated at arrival), but the quote location and the edge the strategy believes it has are.
  • 5. Proposal, not built: a bucketed table in place of the single expected hedge move. Key (all known at quote time): side; distance from the touch in ticks (4 buckets, edges from TRAIN quantiles); Binance 1 s range (4 buckets); queue state (empty level or real queue); optionally Bybit flow sign. Value per bucket: the Binance bid move from quote time to hedge arrival as a curve over the hedge delay (30 ms to 30 s), the fill probability, and the same for Bybit's mid. Where it plugs in: (a) edge.py (core strategies/edge.py after Phase F): expected_hedge = hedge_px * (1 + move) takes the bucket's quote-to-hedge move at the hedge delay instead of the scalar, so the quote is priced for the move that conditional fills carry; (b) the hedge failure cost: retry, unwind and escalation times read the same curve at 250 ms, 1 s and 5 s, replacing p_fail x loss_fail with SCENARIO values labelled with source and TRAIN days (Phase C); (c) the quoting gate: skip a quote when its bucket's expected edge per quote (fill probability times conditional edge, less fees) is below the minimum edge, replacing the uncalibrated toxic thresholds. To validate on SELECT (03-08 to 03-12): fit on TRAIN only; probe or strategy runs on the SELECT days with the same slices; every bucket at least 100 fills in both sets; predicted against realised bucket means within two day-block SEs; the ordering of the buckets by edge must hold out of sample; stability across days; the hedge delay tied to a measured arrival time (45 ms is assumed); more than two hours a day of data (the probe slices are 00:00 to 02:00 UTC only); no use of HOLDOUT, and a refit check because quoting by the table changes which fills occur (the table is fitted on probe quotes at one depth, so the distance buckets must be re-measured when the strategy quotes elsewhere). Limits of this report: Bybit-only probe, Binance L1 only, March stamps constructed (exchange + 2 ms), fees ignored, 7 correlated days, a 15 s order lifetime.

C16. C15 follow-up: does the real strategy show the probe's quote-to-fill drift? (7 Nov; report only; EXPLORATORY)

  • Answer: yes, and it decides S1's priority upward. From the existing strategy runs (no replay): 2022-01-13 v1.1 + message-atomic (19 signals with a maker fill), 2022-01-05 v1.1 (3), March v1_2 slices 03-08 to 03-11 (2; the other slices had none). 24 signals, 12 with the maker on Bybit (hedge on Binance), 12 with the maker on Binance (hedge on Bybit). Script phase_b/strategy_quote_drift.py → strategy_quote_drift.json. Reference = the hedge venue's touch on the side the hedge trades, in the strategy's own view book (at hedge arrival: the truth book); adverse is negative; everything before fees.
  • Quotes are not repriced on Binance moves. Plainly: there is no repricing and no cancel on a hedge-venue move anywhere in the engine. ArbConfig.maker_requote_bps (arb_strategy.py:142, comment “requote if fair moves by this much”) is declared and read by nothing; the backtester itself says so in a startup warning (backtester.py:488-495: “The engine has no cancel/replace on price moves (maker_requote_bps is not used)”). A resting quote leaves the book in only three ways: a fill; the time-in-force cancel at placed_ts + maker_max_age_s (_expire_maker_orders, backtester.py:2581-2617; 15 s in the v1.1 and v1_2 profiles, maker_max_age_s: 15.0, MANDATORY); or a circuit-breaker trip (backtester.py:1502, 2658). maker_max_live_orders is 1, so while a quote rests the strategy cannot quote again; after a fill or the 15 s cancel the next signal builds a new quote from the then-current Binance bid. So a quote that Binance has left behind stays where it is for up to 15 s plus the cancel latency, and fills exactly when the market comes down to it.
  • Age of the quote at fill (decision to fill report): median 2.2 s, mean 4.1 s, longest 14.8 s; 7 of 24 older than 5 s. Bybit-maker fills: mean 6.4 s (14.0, 9.0, 13.2, 14.8 and 7.0 s among them); Binance-maker fills: mean 1.7 s.
  • Drift and edge, means (SE over the 24 signals): hedge-venue price quote → fill −4.80 bps (0.89), median −4.15; fill → hedge arrival +0.18 (the arrival is tens of milliseconds after the fill, so almost nothing moves); quote → hedge arrival −4.62. 18 of 24 fills drifted against the quote and 13 of them by 3 bps or more; none drifted in its favour. The edge the strategy believed at quoting (before fees, with its own expected hedge move) was +8.15 bps (0.29); the realised hedge edge before fees was +3.83 (1.02); shortfall −4.31 (0.89); 5 of 24 realised below zero. After the actual fees of each pair: −2.17 bps (0.89) per filled signal (the strategy's own believed conditional net edge was +1.13 and +1.25 bps in the two January runs; p_fail and loss_fail are not in this figure).
    Bybit-maker fills (n 12): drift −6.17 (1.26), believed +6.71, realised +1.08, net after fees −3.42. Binance-maker fills (n 12): drift −3.43, believed +9.59, realised +6.59, net −0.91. The scalar the strategy used for the expected hedge move was −0.75 / −0.33 bps (January 01-13), 0 (01-05) and −0.49 / −0.15 (March v1_2): it covers a small part of a −4.8 bps average. The summaries' own legging reports agree: realised hedge move −5.03 bps (n 19) on 01-13, −2.94 (n 3) on 01-05, −8.04 (n 1) on March 03-11.
  • Compared with the probe: the same sign and the same mechanism. The probe quoted 8.5 bps deep and drifted −8.3 bps before its fills; the strategy's quote cushion is its believed gross edge (6.7 bps for a Bybit maker, 9.6 for a Binance maker), and the fills arrive after the hedge venue has given back a large part of it (−6.2 and −3.4 bps). The fills are the moments when the cushion is gone.
  • Limits. 24 signals, 22 of them from two January days, 2 from March; no confidence interval beyond the SEs above; the hedge-venue reference is the strategy's feed-delayed view at the decision and at the fill (the delay is 36 ms for Binance, small against a multi-bps drift); fees as in the runs; HOLDOUT untouched.

C17. Final v1_2 cells: ledger, stamps, placement book, four-model range (7 Nov; March slices 03-08 to 03-11, 00:00-02:00 UTC; EXPLORATORY: size 1, singapore, fees ASSUMED)

Final v1_2 = the committed profile: March windows 0.15 s / 50 ms applied by the runner, placement_book view, through rule off, ledger on, stamps untrusted (cell ft). Cells: f0 / f1 = ledger off / on with the March stamps trusted; fp = ft with placement_book: truth; fbf / faf / fco = ft with behind_first / ahead_first / cohort uniform (the profile's cross rule on). 28 runs, all rc 0. Script phase_b/final_cells_report.py → ledger/final_cells_report.json. The sample is three orders: treat every line as a wiring result, not a measurement.

  • Headline ft: 3 signals, 3 maker fills (all cause trade), cash P&L −0.0606, gross +0.068, fees 0.1286 (03-09: 2 fills, cash −0.0064, 30 s markout +1.14 bps; 03-11: 1 fill, cash −0.0542, −1.09 bps; 03-08 and 03-10: no signal).
  • Ledger (f1 against f0): files and results identical on all four slices; the only difference is the depletion_ledger key in the settings dump. No print was ever netted against a decrease at one of our levels, so the ledger has nothing to act on (as in C2).
  • Stamps (ft against f1): large effect. With the constructed 2 ms stamps used as they are (f1): 7 signals, 2 fills, cash −0.0554; with the preset feed delay (ft): 3 signals, 3 fills, cash −0.0606. The strategy rejects more on a stale Binance view (signals_blocked_by_book_stale_binance 22 against 27 on 03-09) and its quote timing shifts, so the number and the timing of orders change. The assumption (ASSUMED, owner 6 Nov) is the largest single driver of the cell results; it stays on the freeze list as an assumption, not a measurement.
  • March windows (ck, the old windows, against ft): results identical on all four slices; only the two window keys differ. The wider windows change nothing here (no netting occurred).
  • Placement book truth alone (fp against ft): identical on all four slices. The switch was active (all 3 orders were placed on the truth book, observation placement_book: truth) and the truth level equalled the view level for each, so the queue ahead was the same (45.0 for one, 0 for the other two). No effect measured; with 3 orders no effect could be.
  • Four-model range: behind_first, ahead_first and cohort uniform give the same trades, fills, P&L and markout as the headline proportional model on every slice (only the model setting differs). The orders rest on empty levels or ahead 0, so there is no queue for a model to move. Range: zero, on this data. This is the same finding as C15: 92 % of probe orders and most strategy orders rest on empty levels.
  • January: v1_2 cannot run on January (its hedge-move pin needs earlier days), and the January v1.1 cells do not read the v1_2 profile; they are unchanged by these decisions and were not rerun.

C18. Freeze proposal for v1_2 (not frozen; awaiting your approval)

  • What would be frozen: v1_2 as the simulator-correct baseline, strategy unchanged. v1_2 = v1_1 (byte-identical control) plus: the join-the-touch switch (arb.join_touch_on_tick_rounding); the probe hedge-move pin (7-day, 45 ms arrival estimate, sha256 c369b1b9…8de7, −0.488 / −0.146 bps); timing floors 35 ms on the Binance entry and cancel timelines (ASSUMED); message_atomic_book; hedge_faults.query_before_replace; A9 above_last_tier; maker.depletion_ledger (on; no netting seen); trust_local_ts false for both venues (ASSUMED); dataset_settings.mar2024.maker (dedupe_window_s 0.15, evidence_window_ms 50); placement_book: view (the truth switch is built and measured inert, left off). Present in the file but OFF and not part of the freeze: maker.fill_through_prints and maker.through_print_volume_limited (false), the cohort queue (a sensitivity case only), placement_book: truth.
  • Release identity to record (existing release_identity fields plus a freeze block): profile sha256 (today 734d7d2c…368a46); code_hash (sources sha256 over 89 files, today 2156a42a…6b5); outer and core git commits and tags (v1_2-freeze in both repos; today outer a0445e3, core 3569d0b, plus the docs commit that carries this report); the hedge-move file sha256; the data manifest sha256 (manifest_mar2024.json f1b6eee8…) and the unknown-queue fit file; the fee snapshot and fee presets; decision_status labels carried as they are (march_stamps ASSUMED; hedge_move PROBE_ARRIVAL_ESTIMATE_45MS_PINNED_BY_SHA256_EXPLORATORY; p_fail PLACEHOLDER_NOT_MEASURED, to be relabelled SCENARIO in Phase C; queue_parameters SPOT_PRIOR_PERP_UNVALIDATED; unwind and breaker DECISION_PENDING_CALIBRATION); the evidence: the test baseline (92 failed + 1 error, identical by name to Phase 0), the byte-identity gate (13 clean + 4 faulted against b1 / f0), the ck against t1 identity, and this report (C1 to C18). I would add a freeze manifest file beside the profile and a test that pins the v1_2 profile sha256, as the earlier profiles are pinned.
  • Known limits that travel with the freeze (stated in every result): the strategy never reprices a resting quote (S0); conditional edge uses a scalar hedge move that leaves out the quote-to-fill drift (S1); the print-first leftover (a decrease larger than its print, ratio below 1.2) is not order-independent; hidden liquidity ahead of us and RPI orders are not modelled; 79 % to 92 % of orders rest on empty levels, so the queue models, the ledger and the placement book are inert on today's data; stamps, latency preset and fees are ASSUMED; March slices are 2 hours with Binance L1 only and no funding file; p_fail and loss_fail_bps are placeholders / simulated scenarios; cohort and the through rule are not validated on perp data.
  • To resolve before the freeze (your decisions): (1) the core repository's working tree carries 37 tracked-file changes that are not mine (staged deletions of advanced_backtest/ and old documents): a frozen release identity would record dirty; commit them as a separate change, restore them, or tell me to leave and record it; (2) the outer repository carries the docs of this turn (report, plan, prompt, runner, scripts): one more commit before the freeze; (3) placement_book: freeze it as view (my recommendation: no measured effect, the January placement cell was not run, v1_3 can carry it) or as truth (consistent with PostOnly); (4) confirm that the through rule stays out; (5) confirm the freeze tag name and that CLAUDE.md should then say v1_2 is frozen and new work goes on v1_3 (S0).

C19. S0 design: quote repricing as profile v1_3 (7 Nov; design only, report only; nothing built)

  • Source of the mechanics and what it is. FAST_MM/Fast_MM_Codebase/bybit_order_manager.py (update_quotes, amend_order). It is a spot altcoin market maker ("category": "spot" in its amend request, inventory exit logic, tranche ladders), not the perp arbitrage bot; I took mechanics only and none of its numbers. I did not open check_link_orders_history.py. Its own header notes: “Amending resets queue priority on Bybit”.
    FAST_MM mechanic (function)TakeDo not take
    Amend first (amend_order: atomic price change, no book gap, one call), cancel-and-replace when the amend is rejected (returns False; “not exist / already” is treated as done and the caller resyncs)Yes: amend first; on rejection cancel and place again only if the edge still passes at the current hedge price; a rejection because the order is gone is a fill or cancel to resync, never a resend (OMS03)Its REST code, its error codes
    Stickiness band (skip an amend when the move is below a band; a Kalman band replaces the fixed one)Yes: a band, separate for adverse and favourable moves1.5 / 3 ticks; the Kalman filter
    Debounce (a move must persist, tick-stability filter; a regime-flip debounce; a cooldown; a cap of amends per pair per tick)Yes: a debounce after a jump, applied to favourable moves only, and a minimum interval between repricesIts 2-tick persistence, its 2 amends per tick
    Maximum quote age (dynamic age cancel and replace), with the queue tracker owning the lifecycle (queue_hold skips the age cancel)Yes: a maximum quote age (replaces the 15 s time in force); a queue hold: an order with a real queue ahead keeps its place against favourable moves20 s, 90 to 120 s
    Directional lock, average-cost floor on amend, tranche ladders, inventory exitNo: they exist because that bot holds inventory; ours hedges every fillAll
  • Where it lives. In the core EMS passive tactic (crypto_stat_arb/ems/tactics/passive.py), as one pure policy: inputs = the resting order (price, age, queue ahead, last reprice time, pending state), the current hedge-venue touch and the own-venue touch; output = HOLD, AMEND(price), CANCEL, or CANCEL_REPLACE. The target price comes from the same edge function as the first placement (edge.py, one place), evaluated at the current hedge-venue (Binance) price, not at Bybit's mid. The backtester's execution layer calls it (new_backtest imports the core, never the reverse) and live calls the same code. No strategy setting may be declared and unused: a test fails on any such field. Today two exist: ArbConfig.maker_requote_bps and the EMS config's primary["reprice"] (declared “none”, read by nothing); both are removed or implemented. The core has no maker repricing today.
  • Rule. edge_now = the edge of the resting price recomputed at the current hedge price; shortfall = required edge − edge_now. Adverse move (shortfall above the band B_down): act at once, subject to the minimum interval M: amend down to the new target; a quote with negative edge is never held for its queue. Favourable move (the target is above the resting price by more than B_up): act only after the debounce D has passed without the target retreating, and not at all while the order has a real queue ahead (queue hold). Age: a quote older than A is cancelled and re-quoted. Rate limits: every amend goes through the core scoped limiter (rate_limits.py, REPLACE cost; 20 % of capacity stays for cancels, EMS02); when an amend does not fit, a cancel is chosen over a stale quote.
  • Cancel latency, the race and the amend model. An amend loses queue priority, as on Bybit: the backtester's MakerExecutor.amend (fill_engine.py:827) already models it (the old order and its place are gone, PostOnly is checked at the amend's arrival); it is never called today and refuses the cohort queue, so S0 runs on the scalar queue. The old order stays resting and fillable until the amend lands at the exchange (the existing cancel-pending semantics): a fill in that window is kept and hedged as any fill (catalogue H30); an amend that finds the order filled is rejected and handled as a fill, not as a failure. The reaction time is the Binance feed delay (36 ms in the singapore preset) plus decision plus the Bybit amend transit; the 35 ms floor applies to the Binance entry and cancel timelines (the hedge, and a Binance-maker amend).
  • Parameters and how they are fitted. B_down, B_up, jump size J and debounce D, minimum interval M, maximum age A, and the queue-hold rule are fitted on the 7 TRAIN probe days (leave-one-day-out inside TRAIN; objective: realised hedge edge net of fees per quote-second, with the cost of lost priority, subject to the rate-limit cap below) and validated on SELECT (03-08 to 03-12) only; HOLDOUT untouched. None of FAST_MM's numbers is used. The fit needs the build (a repricing mode in the probe), so no value is proposed here.
  • Reprice rate against both venues' limits (design estimate, not a fit: b0/s0_reprice_rate_estimate.py → probe_adverse/s0_reprice_rate_estimate.json; the 3,213 probe orders of the 7 TRAIN days, 40,016 live seconds, a quote that follows the Binance bid with the singapore feed delay; one live order at a time). Limits from the core capabilities table: Bybit PERP IP 600 requests / 5 s (documented), /v5/order/amend 10 / s, create 10 / s, cancel 10 / s (assumed); Binance PERP orders 300 / 10 s and 1,200 / 60 s, weight 2,400 / 60 s (assumed).
    band (down) / min interval / up movesamends per order (mean, p90, max)per live secondpeak in any 1 speak in any 5 s
    0.5 bps / none / two-sided34.3, 65, 4242.7684212
    0.5 bps / 0.25 s / two-sided19.3, 33, 541.55521
    1 bps / 0.25 s / down only2.6, 6, 100.21411
    2 bps / 0.25 s / down only1.5, 3, 60.1247
    3 bps / 0.25 s / down only1.0, 2, 40.0845
    5 bps / 0.25 s / down only0.5, 1, 20.0423
    Reading: on average the amend rate is 0.04 to 2.8 per second against Bybit's assumed 10 per second (under 30 % of it even for the tightest two-sided band, under 5 % for bands of 2 bps and wider). The danger is the burst, not the average: with no minimum interval a tight two-sided band reaches 84 amends in one second (8 times the Bybit amend limit) and 212 in five seconds (35 % of the IP bucket); a minimum interval of 0.25 s caps it at 4 per second (40 % of the amend limit) whatever the band. In the Bybit-maker configuration Binance receives only the hedges (one order per fill: negligible against 300 per 10 s); if Binance is the maker venue (the January configuration) its amends count against 300 / 10 s (30 per second) and 1,200 / 60 s (20 per second) and the same minimum interval keeps them far below. One live quote per account, as today: more pairs or more quotes scale these figures linearly.
  • Why the queue hold matters less than in FAST_MM. 92 % of probe orders and most strategy orders rest on an empty level, where an amend loses no queue position of any value; queue hold matters only for the real-queue minority (8 % of probe orders).
  • Measured alone, against frozen v1_2: profile v1_3 = v1_2 plus a requote block, nothing else. Report on the probe (7 TRAIN days) and the strategy runs, then SELECT: quote age at fill, quote-to-fill drift, believed against realised edge, cancel and reprice counts (and fills that arrived inside an amend window), fills, and net P&L after fees. With the block off v1_3 equals v1_2 byte for byte. S1 (the adverse-selection table) is then fitted on the fills of this repricing strategy.
  • Answer from your lead (7 Nov): yes, the live perp arbitrage bot reprices its Bybit quotes when Binance moves. So this is a missing feature in the backtest, not a shared defect, and S0 copies the live rule exactly: trigger, band, debounce, maximum age, amend against cancel-and-replace, queue hold, minimum interval and rate-limit handling, with the live values labelled as live values. I cannot copy what I cannot see: the live rule is not in this repository (Bybit_MM/ and FAST_MM/ are spot makers with no Binance hedge; the core has none). Needed from you: the live function or a written description with its values. The TRAIN fit and the SELECT validation then compare the live rule with alternatives rather than choose its settings; v1_2 stays the frozen baseline without live repricing, and the backtest-versus-live gap is reported explicitly.

C21. S0 built: quote repricing, modular, parameters left to the trader (7 Nov; profile v1_3, off by default)

  • Owner's instruction: the live bot reprices, there is no repricing function in the repository, so use the existing mechanics (FAST_MM, mechanics only) or write a generic function and leave the numbers to the trader. Built as the generic function; no number is set, defaulted or fitted.
  • Modules (each replaceable on its own). (1) The rule, core, pure: crypto_stat_arb/ems/tactics/passive.py: RepriceConfig, RepriceState, decide_reprice, after_amend_rejected. Answers HOLD, AMEND, CANCEL_REPLACE or CANCEL from: stickiness band for adverse moves, a separate band for favourable moves (unset = they never move a quote), queue hold, debounce after a hedge-price jump, minimum interval between requests, maximum quote age, amend first with cancel-and-replace on rejection. A target of None (no edge at the current hedge price) cancels at once, whatever the interval. (2) The target: ArbStrategy.requote_target: the edge recomputed at the current hedge-venue (Binance) price; the price arithmetic was factored into _quote_limit, now the one place for first placement and repricing (bit-identical). (3) The simulator glue: new_backtest/requote.py (Requoter, no rule inside): watches the hedge venue, asks the rule, sends the amend after a sampled entry latency (cancel-and-replace: cancel plus entry), applies it at the exchange with the PostOnly check on the arrival-time book, loses the queue place (MakerExecutor.amend), keeps the old order resting and fillable until then (H30: a fill in the window is kept and hedged, counted), counts everything and writes requote.json. (4) The setting: execution.primary.reprice ("none" or a mapping; it was declared and read by nothing); the new profile perp_perp_maker_taker_v1_3.yaml = v1_2 + that one block, enabled: false, every number null, source TRADER_SET (or LIVE_VALUES, or ILLUSTRATIVE_NOT_FITTED); enabling it without band_down_bps, min_interval_ms and max_age_s is refused.
  • To use it: set enabled: true and the numbers in the v1_3 profile (or the live bot's values, with source: LIVE_VALUES). The source label is carried into the summary and requote.json.
  • No setting declared and unused. maker_requote_bps and maker_improve_ticks (also read by nothing) are removed; a test (NoUnusedSettings) fails on any unread ArbConfig or RepriceConfig field or EMS section key. Listed debt, not fixed because frozen profiles name them: the EMS keys primary.price_rule, hedge.q, maker_maker.escalate_after_s, unwind.fallback and unwind.window_s are read by nothing.
  • Tests: tests/test_requote.py, 24: the rule (band, interval, cancel never delayed, queue hold, debounce, age, amend rejected, validation), the glue in the backtester harness (off builds nothing; an adverse hedge move amends after the latency; inside the band holds; a fill while the amend is in flight is kept; a partial fill in the window is counted; a PostOnly refusal leaves nothing resting; no edge cancels; a stale hedge touch is never acted on; a max-age re-quote starts a new quote), v1_3 differs from v1_2 only in the switch.
  • Smoke run (a wiring check, not a setting and not a result): v1_3 with the rule on and ILLUSTRATIVE numbers (band 2 bps down only, 250 ms interval, 15 s age; not fitted, not recommended) on the four March slices: it ran end to end; 03-09 made 4 amends and 03-11 2, all adverse-move, every amend applied, holds counted (inside_band, pending, min_interval); cash 03-09 +0.0087 against −0.0064 without repricing, 03-11 −0.468 against −0.054. Two orders: it says nothing about whether repricing helps. A defect found by this run and fixed: a max-age re-quote did not reset the quote's age, so it repeated every interval and chased the hedge price up (9 requests in 1.5 s on 03-11, filled at the top of a spike); the age now counts from the last placement or amend, with a test. The pre-fix run is kept as runs/v3on_before_age_fix.
  • Byte identity: v1_2 on the final code equals the frozen-baseline cell on all four slices (files and every summary field); v1_3 with the switch off equals it too, differing only in its identity labels (cell, profile, execution config and its sha). The v1_2 profile file is unchanged (pin test passes). The source hash in the freeze manifest is of the earlier code and is superseded for new work; v1_2's identity is its profile sha256 and its results.
  • Gate and suite after S0: the 13 clean baselines and the 4 faulted runs are byte-identical to the Phase 0 reference (ALL_IDENTICAL: true in both). Full suite: 2,758 passed; the failing list equals the Phase 0 list (92 failed + 1 collection error) plus two tests of mine that S0 had broken (the runner guard that names the profiles refusing the old flag, and the guard that nothing in the engine reads an observation: the amended-quote observation now goes through Backtester._obs_requote_place); both fixed, and the affected test files (60) re-run green; the full suite was not run a second time. The edit after the gate (age reset in requote.py, observation routing) touches only code that runs when the rule is on.

C23. Phase B2, step 2: funding in the shadow (7 Nov; January 2022-01-13, v1.1 + message-atomic book, shadow on; EXPLORATORY)

  • What was built: CoreShadow now runs the core FundingEngine beside the backtester's funding_settler, fed the same funding-rate files (window and venues as the settler), the same fills (through the shadow ledger) and every book mid (on_mid); the engine marks a settlement at the mid prevailing at its time (mark_fallback: book_mid; the files carry no mark price). With funding on, both ledgers book funding into cash, so nothing is taken out of the backtester's side any more. Compared: totals per venue and settlement by settlement (venue, time): position, rate, mark, payment (the backtester's payment is positive when paid, the core's amount is the holder's cash flow).
  • Result, January 01-13 (4 settlements, both venues): every settlement matched; position, rate and mark identical; the largest payment difference is 3.6e-18 USDT; per venue Bybit +0.00043562 and Binance −0.00044437 on both sides; no unmatched settlement either way, no core flag. The sign convention is the same. The overall shadow report has no difference left (positions, trade cash, fees, funding and equity: the equity gap at the core's common-timestamp marks is −6.6e-14; the per-venue last-mid shortcut would have shown 6.45 USDT).
  • Run unchanged: with the shadow on, trades, fill events, fill bucket stats, model settings and every summary field equal the earlier shadow-off January cell. Tests: 2 new (agreement on sign, position and amount; a wrong sign in the backtester is reported), 11 in tests/test_core_shadow.py. March has no funding file: not testable there.
  • What it means for the switch-over: the core engine reproduces the settler's numbers on the one day that has funding, so switching funding to the core (step 5) has a measured precondition. The settlement-timing difference the core allows (settle lag, schedule snapping) did not matter here because the records are on the schedule. Two settlements per venue is a small sample: the switch itself will be measured on January 01-05 as well.

C24. Phase B2, step 3: margin in the shadow (7 Nov; January 2022-01-13 on v1.1 + message-atomic book, March 03-08..03-11 on v1_2; shadow on; EXPLORATORY)

  • What was built: at every periodic check of the backtester's MarginModel (every 1 s) the shadow calls the core RiskEngine.evaluate_margin (dry run, no open orders) on its own ledger: tiered cross margin, the ECONOMIC_LEDGER_COLLATERAL balance from opening balances (initial capital per venue), the position valued at the book mid, A9 fail-closed above the last tier. The model records the numbers it used and its breach decision (last_rows, written and never read by the model); the backtester hands them to the shadow right after the check and before any liquidation fill. Compared per venue: balance against the backtester's equity (after removing the fee difference, running per venue), maintenance and initial margin, tier index and the breach decision. Core tiers are built with named keys (the two classes order maintenance and initial rate differently). The shadow never liquidates and compares only decisions.
  • Result: January 01-13: 84,018 checks evaluated (all with a position; 263 flat checks sampled), 168,036 venue rows, breach decision agreed on every row, tier index agreed, largest balance gap after the fee correction 2.3e-13 USDT, largest maintenance difference 4.6e-16, initial 9.2e-16, no difference of any class. March 03-09 and 03-11 (the days with fills): 5,068 and 4,149 checks, all agreed (gaps 1e-13 and 2e-13); 03-08 and 03-10 have no fills (11 checks each, flat). The earlier shadow sections (positions, trade cash, fees, equity, funding) are identical to the B2.1 and B2.2 outputs on all five runs.
  • Run unchanged: with the shadow on, trades, fill events, fill bucket stats and model settings are byte-identical to the shadow-off cells (l0 January, ft March) and every summary field equals (except wall time and paths). The wall time on January went from 668 s to 679 s (1.6 %). Gate: the 13 clean baselines and the four faulted runs against b1 and f0 are all identical (v1.1, legacy_v0 and frozen v1 included). Full suite: 92 failed, 2,772 passed, 4 skipped, 1 collection error; the failing names equal the Phase 0 list exactly (93 entries, all tests/platform_kit). Tests: 7 new in tests/test_core_shadow.py (18 in the file): same fills agree; equity below maintenance is a breach on both sides at the same check; A9 breach agrees and ignore is BY_DESIGN; a fee difference is removed from the balance and flips a decision only as a C1 difference; a wrong backtester cash is an unclassified balance difference; the evaluation is read-only; funding settled mid-run is in the core balance.
  • Limits, stated plainly: (1) no real check came near maintenance: the largest notional seen is 197 USDT against 1,000 USDT per venue, so the breach path is agreed on real data only as "no breach"; the breach, A9 and fee-flip cases are exercised by the synthetic tests only. A stress run with small capital was not made (a profile copy would be needed; ask if you want it). (2) Not compared: the margin gate (initial margin of a new clip, reserved margin of resting orders), isolated mode, transfers (equity is compared net of transfer cash), liquidation execution and liquidation price. (3) The core funding engine settles when asked: the shadow asks (non-final, one ns before the check) so funding paid mid-run is in the core balance; the final settle still gives the B2.2 result. (4) The core margin result does not depend on the core fee schedule once the fee difference is removed; with the fees as they are, a decision can differ only by C1.
  • What it means for the switch-over: the core margin path reproduces the backtester's margin numbers and decisions to rounding on every real check, so switching margin to the core (step 6) has a measured precondition. The mark used is still the book mid for both; the core also accepts an exchange mark price, which March and January do not carry.
  • New owner instructions recorded (not applied): the fee presets and the headline after B2 (Binance VIP 9 + Bybit VIP 5, 2.7 bps; scenarios 1.2 and 4.5 bps), the one-accounting end state, the funding mark rule (FUNDING_MARK_FALLBACK), the core margin switch with tiers ASSUMED (ask before downloading), the strict-bridge carry-over and the missing-mark flag (MARK_MISSING) are in core_backtest_integration_prompt.md and single_platform_plan.md under B2. They are applied at their steps, each on a profile switch measured alone.

C25. Phase B2, step 4: fees from the core FeeSchedule (7 Nov; profile v1_4; March 03-08..03-11, shadow on; EXPLORATORY)

  • Two separate changes, measured one at a time. 4a, mechanism: new profile perp_perp_maker_taker_v1_4 (v1_3 plus one switch, fee_source: core; v1_2 and v1_3 stay on the backtester presets). With the switch on, the preset names are tier names of the core (crypto_stat_arb.fees.FEE_TIERS); each venue's maker and taker rate is the core schedule's booked rate (FeeSchedule.rate_bps, conservative policy applied), turned into a float once at construction. Every consumer already reads venue_specs[v].fees (edge, quote price, taker fees, maker fills, liquidation, requote target), so there is one rate path. The shadow is built from the same rows. 4b, rates: the VIP tiers as the v1_4 headline.
  • Core changes (core repo): FeeSource.PUBLIC_SCHEDULE_UNVERIFIED (unverified everywhere; conservative_unverified applies; the booked label is ASSUMED_PUBLIC_SCHEDULE_UNVERIFIED, so the ledger still classes the fee as ASSUMED); FEE_TIERS with four tiers and tier_rows / schedule_for (a tier cannot be used on the wrong venue). The tiers are not added to config/perp_research.json (that is the live and paper config, effective-dated, and a later row would silently become the shadow's rate) and not to the backtester FEE_PRESETS.
  • Tiers: bybit_perp_vip5 maker 1.0, taker 3.2; binance_perp_vip9 maker 0.0, taker 1.7 (BNB taker 1.53 recorded, not built: a BNB fee needs a BNB price); both PUBLIC_SCHEDULE_UNVERIFIED, source date «owner-provided 7 Nov 2026» (I do not have the exchange's publication date), account SOLUSDT rates not read. bybit_perp_mm (maker −0.5) is kept as a labelled SCENARIO and credits the rebate on purpose; binance_perp_vip0 (2.0 / 5.0) is the old ASSUMED tier. An unverified negative maker fee books 0 (tested).

With the shadow's schedule built from the run's own rows the fee difference is 0 to 1.9e-18 USDT and no difference is reported. The release identity now carries the rows booked and their hash.
  • 4b result, four SELECT slices, size 1, Singapore latency, EXPLORATORY, handful of trades:

  • How to read it: a lower fee lowers the edge the strategy asks for, so it signals and quotes more (3, 4, 7 signals; 6, 13, 13 orders), and the extra orders fill into the same adverse drift (C15/C16). A few trades per set: these are not P&L figures, they show that the fee set changes how many trades are taken and which ones, not only the cost of each. The headline is not better than the old set on this sample; the 1.2 bps scenario is, and it depends on a rebate that is not confirmed. No HOLDOUT day was used. January cannot run on a v1_2-derived profile (the hedge-move pin needs days before the run start).
