C26. Phase B2, step 5: funding from the core FundingEngine (7 Nov; January 2022-01-13 on v1.1 + message-atomic book with funding_source core; EXPLORATORY)

  • What was built. A new switch funding_source (settler in v1, v1.1, v1_2, v1_3, legacy_v0; core in v1_4). With core, the backtester's settler is a CoreFundingSettler (in core_shadow.py, same interface as FundingSettler, so no consumer changed). At each call every rate row whose time has passed goes to the core FundingEngine, which settles it against the ledger's position at that time (fills strictly before the settlement count); the core's cash flow is applied to the run's cash. funding_settler is never constructed (tested). The core ledger it uses is the shadow's ledger, which therefore becomes the run's accounting object (renamed at step 7).
  • Mark rule (owner 7 Nov), in the core: the funding record's own mark price where present, otherwise the book mid at the settlement, now flagged FUNDING_MARK_FALLBACK (non-blocking) and counted in the funding report (FUNDING_MARK_FALLBACK, mark_sources, mark_basis), which every core-funding result carries. A settlement with no mark at all is flagged FUNDING_MARK_MISSING (blocking, economic status INCOMPLETE) and recorded with payment 0 and source none: never dropped silently (tested). The setting is in the run manifest (release_identity.funding_source, funding_modelled). With no rate directory (March has no funding file) funding is not modelled, as with the settler, and the manifest says so.
  • Result, January 01-13 (cell jc) against the settler cell jsh: trades, fill events, fill bucket stats and model settings are byte-identical and every numeric summary field is equal, including funding_paid (Bybit +0.00043563, Binance −0.00044438). 4 settlements booked by the core, all on the book-mid mark (the SOL funding files carry no mark price): FUNDING_MARK_FALLBACK = 4, mark basis ESTIMATED, economic status COMPLETE. The shadow's own comparison of the core settlements with the cash actually applied: largest payment difference 8.8e-19 USDT; margin shadow still agrees on all 168,036 rows. The run took 11.5 minutes alone (668 s with the settler: the same order).
  • A bug of mine, found by the first run and fixed: the first jc run booked 3 of 4 settlements (funding paid +0.0194 instead of −0.0000088). The due time of a row was kept as a float round trip (ms / 1e3 * 1e9), up to 256 ns below the engine's integer key, so when the timer wheel called exactly at a row with odd milliseconds (Binance 08:00:00.014) the engine's cutoff was just under the row, and the row was already consumed. The due time is now the row's exact integer ns; a regression test calls the settler at exactly such a time. The flawed run is kept as jc_before_due_ns_fix and is not used.
  • Checks: the 13 clean baselines and the four faulted runs against b1 and f0 are identical (the earlier profiles stay on the settler); full suite 92 failed, 2,786 passed, 1 collection error, the failing names equal the Phase 0 list exactly. Tests: 7 new in tests/test_core_shadow.py (33 in the file): booking and flagged mark, once-only booking, the record's own mark (no flag), no mark at all (flagged, INCOMPLETE), no settler class built, odd-millisecond row, no rate directory.
  • Known item (not fixed here): the core report's overall funding status reads INCOMPLETE because the shadow's FundingSpec carries no funding interval (the schedule is unknown: a missing record could not be detected); economic_status is COMPLETE. Giving the instrument its 8-hour schedule would also let the core snap the Binance rows with a few ms of jitter and flag a missing record; it changes what the engine settles, so it is a separate change measured alone. I did not make it now. Funding on January 05 is not yet measured (a 4-settlement sample so far).

C27. Phase B2, step 6: margin decided by the core (7 Nov; March 03-08..03-11 on v1_4, January 01-13 on v1.1 with funding and margin from the core; EXPLORATORY)

  • What was built. A new switch margin_source (model in v1, v1.1, v1_2, v1_3, legacy_v0; core in v1_4). With core the margin model of the run is a CoreMarginModel (in core_shadow.py): every check evaluates the core RiskEngine margin path on the ledger the fills are booked into (tiers, collateral balance from the ledger, cross mode, A9 fail-closed above the last tier, per venue), and a breach is the core's BREACH state; the admission gate is the core's worst-case initial-margin increment against its free margin (_tiered_initial_increment). The old model's decision code (_breaches, equity, its allows) is not called (tested by making them raise). What stays in the backtester's class is bookkeeping (counters, events, the flatten after a liquidation, the report) and the execution of a liquidation against the simulator's book, because the core's own liquidation engine walks a simulated venue adapter the backtester does not have. Margin needs the core ledger to carry the funding the run pays, so margin_source core with funding modelled requires funding_source core (refused otherwise).
  • A9 is redundant under the core: above the last tier the core fails closed whatever above_last_tier says (tested with ignore); the setting stays in the profile and has no effect on the core path.
  • Differences from the old model, sorted: BY_DESIGN (the core rule wins): (1) the admission gate counts worst-case exposure (position plus resting orders plus the clip on top), so a clip that only reduces a position is no longer netted: a test shows a closing clip refused by the core and allowed by the old gate when equity is tight; on every real day no signal was blocked by either. (2) A9 is not optional. NOT MODELLED on the core path, refused at construction, not ignored: transfers, isolated mode, a utilisation limit. NOT PROVIDED by the core, reported as None: the liquidation price and distance. Open, BUG_BACKTEST-side: the liquidation fee rides on the backtester's fill and is not in the core ledger (no liquidation happened in any real run; step 7 books it in the ledger). If any mark is missing at a check the check is skipped and counted (the old model could still check the other venue); one such check on January 01-13.
  • Result on real data: March slices (v1_4 headline fees, cell g1m against g1): trades, fill events, fill bucket stats and model settings are byte-identical on all four days and every scalar summary field is equal; margin statistics equal to rounding (checks 7,011 and 7,078; minimum equity 997.77 and 994.67; peak initial-margin use 0.29 %; minimum margin ratio 678 and 704; no signal blocked, no liquidation). January 01-13 (cell jm against jc, core funding in both): the same four files byte-identical, every scalar equal; 84,282 checks and 102 gate calls on the core, none refused, no breach row, no warning row. No real check came near maintenance (as in C24), so breach and refusal on the core path are agreed only by the synthetic differential tests: same liquidation and flatten orders on a breach, same refusal when equity cannot post the initial margin.
  • Checks: the 13 clean baselines and the four faulted runs against b1 and f0 are all identical (the earlier profiles stay on margin_model). Full suite under load: 95 failed, 2,792 passed, 1 collection error; the failing names equal the Phase 0 list plus the three variants of one timing test in tests/platform_kit/test_minreq_execution.py (test_performance_limits_are_enforced_on_the_target_platform_only), which passes 3 of 3 when rerun alone on the idle machine (as in C25); I did not touch that code. Tests: 8 new in tests/test_core_shadow.py (41 in the file).

C28. Phase B2, step 7: the ledger, the equity bridge and the economics gate from the core (7 Nov; March 03-08..03-11 on v1_4, January 01-13 on v1.1 with every core switch; EXPLORATORY)

  • Three changes, each with its own cell. 7a funding schedule (cell jcs): the shadow's FundingSpec now carries the 8-hour schedule, and the times the engine is asked to settle are the post-snap record times plus every 8-hour boundary of the window, so a boundary with no record is flagged while cash can still change. January 01-13: files and every scalar are identical to the previous core-funding run; the Binance row at 08:00:00.014 now snaps to its boundary; the core funding status reads COMPLETE (it read INCOMPLETE) and FUNDING_RATE_MISSING is 0. A test shows a boundary with no record is flagged per venue and recorded, not dropped. 7b MARK_MISSING (core change, own core commit): Ledger.recalculate_account_state now flags a position that is held with no mark (average price stands in, unrealised 0, as before), returns mark_missing and remembers the distinct VENUE:INSTRUMENT pairs in Ledger.mark_missing (not a per-call count: margin evaluates it at every check); reconcile(mark_missing=...) reports them and is INCOMPLETE; the economics gate lists them under MISSING_PRICE. Flat positions need no mark (tested). 7c ledger_source: core (v1_4): below.
  • 7c, what the switch does. Headline ending equity and cash P&L are the core ledger's at the common-timestamp marks (the venue mids, flagged, when no common timestamp exists). The core now holds the backtester's strict bridge as economics.strict_bridge (Decimal), with every old check at the same tolerances: trade-log rebuild of cash and position per venue against the ledger; each fill's fee against the schedule's booked rate times notional, plus the declared liquidation fee on a liquidation; equity against cash plus the open position at the common timestamp; and one new check the old bridge never needed: the float inventory the strategy decides on against the ledger, per venue. The run then writes economic_reconciliation.json, funding_report.json and data_quality_report.json to its directory and calls the core economics_gate; a failed bridge or a failed gate stops the run unless equity_bridge is warn, as before. The old _equity_bridge is not called (tested by making it raise). Liquidation fills are booked in the core ledger as LIQUIDATION with the declared fee on top of the taker fee (closes the open item of C27; no real run has a liquidation, a synthetic test covers it). The ledger switch needs a margin configuration (the core ledger's opening balances) and, with funding modelled, funding_source core (refused otherwise).
  • Result. March slices, cell g1l against g1m (same profile before the ledger switch): trades, fill events, fill bucket stats and model settings are byte-identical on all four days; the headline scalars differ only because their source changed, by at most 3.6e-14 USDT (ending equity and cash P&L); bridge OK on all four (equity gap 0 to 3.6e-14), economics gate PASS, core reconciliation RECONCILED. January 01-13, cell jl (funding, margin and ledger all from the core) against jm: the same four files byte-identical; cash P&L and equity differ by 5e-15 and 6.6e-14; bridge OK, gate PASS, RECONCILED; the four funding settlements are listed as ESTIMATED (book mid used as the mark), the one thing the gate reports that the old bridge did not.
  • Two things the first run showed, and what I did. (1) The core reconciliation's exact identity check failed by 4e-25 USDT on a float-fed ledger: quantities from binary floats have 17 digits and Decimal's 28-digit context rounds products at that size. reconcile now takes identity_tolerance (default 0: the core's behaviour is unchanged, exact for quantities on an exchange step); the backtester passes 1e-15 and the tolerance is printed in the check's detail, never silent (tested both ways). The first run is kept as g1l_identity_too_strict. (2) The gate's DATA_QUALITY check only sees preflight rejections; a manifest gap (January's truncated tape) is a separate gate with its own written reason, so the bridge document now carries both the preflight result and the manifest reason (data_quality in equity_bridge), so it cannot pass unseen. Real runs: preflight HEALTHY, no rejected venue, manifest reason recorded.
  • Not done, said plainly: (a) the float inventory still drives the strategy; the core ledger is fed the same fills and checked against it, it does not replace it. (b) _exec_reconcile (the execution layer's own reconciliation) still reads the float cash. (c) trade_pnl_by_venue and legacy_fees_by_venue are not passed to reconcile (exact Decimal equality against float figures); the bridge's fee check covers fees with a tolerance. (d) No real run held a position without a mark (mark_missing is empty on all runs). (e) The module is still core_shadow.py, now holding the run's accounting object, CoreFundingSettler, CoreMarginModel and the shadow itself: renaming it is a new file under the project rule, so it is listed for you at the B2 gate rather than done.
  • Checks: the 13 clean baselines and the four faulted runs against b1 and f0 are all identical (every earlier profile stays on the backtester's own books; the core ledger never runs on them). Full suite under load: 93 failed, 2,806 passed, 1 collection error; the failing names equal the Phase 0 list plus one test (tests/test_perp_arb_legs.py::AccountingTests::test_markouts_are_diagnostics_and_never_enter_realized_pnl) that reads _build_result's source with inspect and failed only because I edited backtester.py while the suite was running; it passes alone on the final code. Tests: 9 new in tests/test_core_shadow.py (50 in the file) and 3 in tests/test_margin_tiers.py (23 in that file).

  • Rule (owner, 7 Nov). A WORKING profile with any of fee_source, funding_source, margin_source, ledger_source not equal to core is refused with a ProfileError, at profile load (apply_profile, so an override is caught too) and again at the top of CrossVenueArbBacktester.__init__ (so a config edited after the load is caught). A profile name that is not in the registry, such as a future v1_5, is WORKING. release_identity now carries profile_status. The three runners qv2_march_run.py, qv2_run.py and p1_run.py print profile: NAME [REFERENCE_ONLY] (v1.1: [CONTROL]) to stdout; nothing is added to a result file.

I disabled the guard once to confirm three of the tests fail without it. Three older tests built a working profile on old modules and were changed to match the rule (a made-up profile name in test_wp7_profiles.py, a funding_source="settler" override on v1_4 in two tests in test_core_shadow.py).

The new tests and every test file that touches profiles.py or the backtester start (core_shadow, data_checks, wp7_profiles, audit_decision_a, perp_fault_presets, execution_layer, latency_preset_guard, requote, order_state_parity, integration_scenarios, r12_breaker, margin_model, funding_settler): 351 passed, 2 skipped, 10 xfailed, 0 failed.

The converter's own count of messages that end crossed is 122 on 2022-01-05 and 9 on 2022-01-13 (the same frame rule and the same numbers as Phase B's B3 scan). The core book counted 82 and 9: it counts crossing episodes. It invalidates the book at the first crossed message and buffers the deltas after it without validating them, so 40 of the 122 crossed messages fell inside episodes that were already invalid. The 82 episodes match the 82 resyncs by data snapshot (each episode ended at the data's own next snapshot); on 2022-01-13 both numbers are 9. Parts a to c are offline. Until D7 c passes, core paper fill estimates are untrusted.

Lot steps are the venues' published current rules (the dates' own steps are not published). Phase D, D5 parity result (9 Nov 2026): v1_5 on the session against v1_4 on the backtester's loop

C49. Parity, every difference classified with a reference (EXPLORATORY; size 1 SOL; fees from the profile; funding not modelled; January is "parity only, not a strategy result")

Both paths run the same strategy, the same profile timing (latency preset, the 35 ms floor, truth books at arrival, fill-report latency), the same maker executor fed the same calls, the same maker lifetime (15 s) and hedge cap (touch slack). The session adds the core's gates (book age 500 ms by the any-message rule, price sanity 500 bps, time integrity with host-offset correction, feed control that cancels resting orders on a stale or invalid book) and the core's lot rule. Lot steps are the venues' published current rules (Binance SOLUSDT perpetual 0.01, Bybit 0.1, fetched 8 Nov, file config/instrument_rules_solusdt_perp.json); current, not historical.

Phase D, D5 after the owner correction of 9 Nov

C48. Owner correction of 9 Nov: the same timing and gates on both runtimes (D5, part 1: timing; part 2: the gate table)

  • The rule. Only the clock, the market data source and the execution adapter may differ between runtimes. Latency, timing and the book and data gates are rules and are the same on both paths. The first D5 run used the core's own latency model and gates and was stopped before it was classified; nothing from it is used.
  • Timing (built, committed D5b: core 98ea03d, outer 5187842). TimelinesLatencyModel (core, simulation/timing.py) draws the router's delays from the SAME Timelines the backtester uses (profile timing: latency preset, the 35 ms Binance floor, jitter, one stream per venue and timeline). Stage map: entry to the entry timeline; cancel to cancel; a PostOnly reject notice to ack_report; a cancel report to cancel_report; a resting fill is known at max(print received, exchange time + fill_report) (the rule in the backtester's _on_fill); strategy, risk, accepted-order ack and an IOC fill report are 0, as on the backtester's loop (owner question Q-T1: v1_4 has no such delays; a non-zero value would change BOTH runtimes). The session driver installs the same clock and feed sampler as the backtester (_install_timing) and applies the profile's trust_local_ts, so receive times are the same draws. In-flight orders land and timers fire BEFORE an event is applied. Truth books at arrival are one shared rule: timing.TruthBooks (hold events ahead per venue, catch the truth book up to the arrival's exchange time, completeness test, fall back to the view when not ready, counted) was moved out of the backtester, which now delegates to it (identity held, tag d5t). A hedge executes against the truth book at its arrival (a throwaway core copy; core_book_from_float), and PostOnly is judged on the truth book when it is complete.
  • Per-order check, synthetic. The same synthetic files through v1_4 on the backtester's loop and v1_5 on the session (tests/test_session_parity_synthetic.py): decision, send, arrival and fill times of both orders agree within the 1 ms tolerance (identical to the microsecond for the maker order: decision and send 1.037192 s, arrival 1.045049 s, fill 3.003271 s; the hedge is sent at the fill and arrives 35 ms later on Binance, the floor). One 1 ns artefact: the router stamps each send one nanosecond after the decision (Q-T2, harmless).
  • Defects found while wiring and fixed: the queue adapter updated the shared per-venue market state a second time on every print; the first Opportunity carried no source event ids and no eligibility fingerprint, so the router rejected every plan as "market state changed" (strategy shim, D5a).

C45. D4 (built, not committed; its parts were measured together, not each alone)

  • Feed converter (the one new source file you approved: new_backtest/canonical_feed.py; tests tests/test_canonical_feed.py, 7 tests). It turns the cross-venue feed into the canonical MarketEvent and TradeEvent: a snapshot record is a BOOK_SNAPSHOT; the deltas of one exchange message are one BOOK_DELTA with each changed level's NEW absolute size, the hybrid rule (add and modify absolute, cancel decremental) run on exact numbers; a trade record is a TradeEvent. Prices and sizes come from the text: an explicit parameter decimal_numbers (default off) on iter_venue_events, _stream_dir, _iter_jsonl_gz and CrossVenueFeed reads the JSON numbers as Decimals; there is no global switch (a first version used one; it stayed set while the converter was suspended between events, which a test now rules out: pull one event from a suspended converter, read the plain loader in that window, get floats, abandon the converter, still floats). The converter refuses a record whose numbers are floats. Reuse of the live adapters: only their envelope (MarketEvent fields, stable_id ids, quality flags): normalize_bybit_depth and normalize_binance_depth read native websocket payloads (data.b, u, E) that these records are not. Difference recorded: the files carry no update ids, so each venue gets a CONSTRUCTED contiguous sequence (flag SEQUENCE_CONSTRUCTED; ids not in the file: TRADE_ID_CONSTRUCTED), so the core book can never see a gap, which the raw data can neither prove nor disprove.
  • One grouping rule. The message rule (same venue, same exchange time, receive stamps within 1 microsecond, another venue's event inside the run held until after it) is message_frames in canonical_feed.py; CrossVenueArbBacktester._mark_frames now runs it (a test calls the real method on a mixed-venue stream: same frames, same last-of-message flags, same moved-event count), and _SAME_STAMP_S is imported from there. This touches the reference path, so the identity check was rerun after it (below).
  • Check against the backtester's book (Bybit 2024-03-01, first 120,000 records): 1,063 book frames; the core OrderBook built from the converted events and the backtester's float book (message-atomic) hold the same ten levels on both sides at 214 checkpoints (prices equal, worst size difference 0). One of the 1,063 frames left the core book not VALID. That finding was WRONG, retracted in C47: it was my test cutting the 120,000 records through the middle of a message (the cut kept 53 of that message's 136 deltas, so the book was crossed half-way through it). Complete messages cross nowhere, as Phase B's scan said. The test now cuts at a message boundary.
  • Runtime switch and v1_5. ArbBacktestConfig.runtime (default backtester), profile switch runtime, profile perp_perp_maker_taker_v1_5.yaml = v1_4 + runtime: session (a test shows the two differ in name, description and runtime only; the file is gitignored and needs git add -f). The reference profiles refuse session at load and at start; an unknown value is refused; a v1_5 run is refused at start with "not wired yet" until D5. The release identity carries runtime. 5 tests in test_core_accounting.py.
  • maker_venue from the profile. TradingSession takes dispatch_policy and maker_venues (defaults unchanged); under FILL_TRIGGERED it passes the first listed maker venue the opportunity trades on to oms.execute and refuses (never guesses) when none is. 3 tests in test_fill_triggered.py.
  • Arrival time (a defect in D3, found by checking before wiring). The router delivers a delayed submit with the book captured at send and the request's CREATION time; the venue time is only the report stamp. The queue adapter used the creation time as placement time and arrival time, which would have put every v1_5 maker order in the queue too early and run the pre-arrival gate wrongly. Fix: the router now calls an adapter's optional handle_at(request, book, arrival_ts_ns) (the deterministic adapter has none, so nothing changes for it); the queue adapter places and cancels at arrival. The book passed is the live core book object, current at arrival. A router test with a 10 ms entry latency shows the order placed at creation + 10 ms and a cancel landing 10 ms after its creation.
  • The arb strategy as a TradingSession strategy (the shim ArbSessionStrategy inside strategies/arb_strategy.py, no new file; tests tests/test_arb_session_strategy.py, 6 tests, 25 s). It wraps CrossVenueArbStrategy unchanged: inventory and cash are read from the core ledger (ledger.positions, ledger.cash_deltas), pending exposure and the live maker count from the OMS plans (open children), books are float mirrors of the core books (float_mirror in simulation/order_book.py, the one Decimal-to-float conversion, shared with the queue adapter) stamped with the core book's own last receive time so the strategy's staleness check sees the real age, the market state is owned by the runtime. The session-time conversions use integer nanosecond division (dividing by 1e9 loses about 100 ns at today's epoch; it showed up as a test failure).
  • Signal to Opportunity. Float to Decimal goes through the shortest round-trip text, so it is lossless; the only rounding is the instrument's tick (a bid rests lower, an ask higher, never through its limit) and step (size down). Real signals: the strategy's own signals on the 2024-03-11 two-hour slice (Bybit and Binance, v1_4's strategy settings, every tenth event): 300 signals, 0 changed by the conversion (venues, direction, size, prices, edge bps), 0 grid snaps. Synthetic tests cover the off-grid case. This is the strategy's signal stream before any breaker or in-flight gate of a run; it proves the conversion, not a result.
  • Quick checks. 558 tests passed, 2 skipped, 5 xfailed (the touched files and the strategy, OMS, hedge, timing, book-gate and integration tests). The import-direction test passes (3 passed): the strategy_mm folder is no longer in crypto_stat_arb (the owner moved it; I did not touch it). March 2024-03-11 identity after ALL the D4 changes (loader parameter, shared grouping rule, router hook, order-book helper, profiles, session), solo: legacy_v0, v1, v1.1 against b1 ALL_IDENTICAL; v1_2 (ft) and v1_4 (g1l) byte-identical in the four files (compare_b1_d4b_m.log, compare_ledger_c2_d4b.log; the earlier d4 run covered only the first part).
  • Parity tolerances, written before any run (prompt, PHASE D): signals equal in count and within 1 ms; orders equal in count, side and quantity, limit price within 1 tick; fills equal in count, within 1 ms and 1 tick; P&L within 0.05 USDT per slice at size 1 SOL; every difference classified rounding, BUG_BACKTEST, BUG_CORE or BY_DESIGN and written into the run manifest.
  • Not built yet: D5 (the runtime glue that feeds the converted events to a TradingSession with the queue adapter, the runner switch, the parity runs and the manifest; the session's own gates replace the backtester's dead-feed, book-state, alarm and breaker gates, each a difference to classify) and D6 (_exec_reconcile reading the ledger; trade_pnl_by_venue and legacy_fees_by_venue into reconcile). The strategy side of the exit condition (no float inventory or cash read by the strategy) is met by the shim; the backtester's own loop still reads them until v1_5 replaces it, and the reconciliation side is D6. The full suite and the 13 + 4 replays are for the Phase D gate.

C47. Owner review of 8 Nov: the crossed message, the invalid-book rule, synthetic sequences (before D5)

  • 1. The crossed message was my test's cut, not a difference in grouping. Phase B's scan (B3b) found 0 books crossed at message end in 862,315 Bybit messages on 2024-03-01; the converter test found 1 in 1,063. I ran the scan's rule (exact exchange and receive stamps), the shared frame rule and the test's own wrapping on the same 120,000 records: all three give the same 1,062 complete messages and one cross, in message 1,062, the last. The records after the cut belong to the same exchange message (83 more deltas), so the book was crossed half-way through it. Grouping and snapshot handling do not differ. Fix: the test now keeps reading to the end of the message it cut (1,063 frames, 215 checkpoints, 0 not VALID, 0 crossed). The earlier "BY_DESIGN, a sign of the recorder" reading in C45 is withdrawn.
  • One frame rule, in one function. The scan and the backtester spelled the rule differently (exact receive stamp against within 1 microsecond). canonical_feed.continues_message is now the only definition: message_frames (backtester and converter) and quality_preflight.scan_venue_day_checks both call it. Re-run on Bybit 2022-01-05 (receive stamps are real there): 1,724,134 messages, 1,383,274 with more than one delta, 122 crossed at message end, exactly the Phase B numbers. A test builds a file with stamps 0.5 and 5 microseconds apart and checks that the scan and the frames both count 3 messages. Crosses do exist in the January Bybit data (122 on 01-05, 9 on 01-13), so the next item matters there.
  • 2. Invalid-book policy on the session path: one rule, in the core. TradingSession(resync_delay_ns, resync_source). A book that is GAP_DETECTED, CHECKSUM_FAILED or INVALID_DATA (the states only a snapshot leaves) starts a timer. The book gate already blocks trading and cancels on any non-VALID book, so trading is suspended from that moment. When the first event of either venue arrives at or after invalid time + delay, the venue is resubscribed: the book is reset and a fresh snapshot is applied. The snapshot comes from the replay feed (CanonicalFeed.resync_snapshot): the data's own state after every record read so far (nothing later than the resync time), repaired of a cross by the backtester book's rule (_uncross, repeated on exact levels; the converter keeps its levels repaired, so the snapshot equals the backtester's book, tested on a crossed message), at the latest constructed sequence so the deltas after it chain on, flagged RESYNC_SIMULATED. If a real snapshot of the data comes first, it ends the wait. If the source has nothing to give, the wait repeats and is logged. PRICE_ANOMALY and STALE end by themselves and are not resynced. The delay is ASSUMED: 5 s (profile setting resync_delay_s in v1_5; the live feed's reconnect backoff is 1 s, so this adds a snapshot round trip and the start-up gate; longer loses more opportunity, so it errs against the strategy). It is labelled ASSUMED in the report and in the release identity; 1 s and 15 s are separate sensitivity runs. The rest of a slice is never frozen. Off (None) in live and for every existing caller.
  • Suspended seconds in every result. BookIntegrityGate now measures every interval it blocked, split at UTC midnight and named by cause (BYBIT:CHECKSUM_FAILED, start-up included), observation only. TradingSession.book_policy_report returns the policy, every resync (invalid since, resynced at, wait, reason, who ended it), the suspended seconds by day and cause, and a total per day (a cause that changes mid-suspension splits the interval across keys, so the total is the number to quote). The D5 run manifest carries it.
  • Same rule in the parity comparison. The backtester runtime repairs a cross at once and never suspends (v1_4 stays byte-identical, so it cannot take the rule). The comparison therefore applies the rule on the comparison side: every difference between v1_5 and v1_4 is first tested against the v1_5 suspension windows from the manifest. A signal, order or fill that v1_4 has inside a suspension window and v1_5 lacks is classified BY_DESIGN (invalid-book policy); anything outside a window is a bug candidate. The windows and the count of crossed messages per day are in the manifest. On the March slices I expect none (0 crossed messages at message end in all four slice days, 72k messages each), so the comparison there is clean; the January days carry the crosses.
  • 3. Constructed sequence numbers. CanonicalFeed.manifest() states sequence_numbers: SYNTHETIC and sequence_gaps: NOT_MEASURED with count null and the reason (the files carry no update ids, so a gap could never show and zero would mean nothing). The D5 manifest takes the gap figure from this, never from the core book's own gaps counter, which is 0 by construction here. Trade ids are labelled constructed where the record has none. Phase B's data checks already say NOT_MEASURED for gaps and duplicates.
  • No later information, enforced (advisor review). resync_snapshot raises if it is asked about a time earlier than the book events the feed has already handed out for that venue (1 microsecond tolerance for an other-venue event held inside a message), so converting everything first and replaying after cannot give a resync the end-of-slice book. A test builds that eager pattern and expects the refusal. The gate also keeps its suspension windows (start, end, cause) and the report carries them: they are what the parity comparison masks.
  • Identity after these changes (tag d5a). March 2024-03-11: legacy_v0, v1, v1.1 identical to b1; the v1_2 and v1_4 cells byte-identical (trades, fill events, bucket stats, model settings).
  • Tests. 2 in test_book_gate.py (suspension split by day and cause, start-up wait), 5 in test_canonical_feed.py (shared frame rule in the scan, wait then resync and counted seconds, resynced book equals the backtester's repaired book with no later information, no policy = invalid for the rest of the slice, delay and source go together, manifest labels), the profile pin updated (v1_5 differs from v1_4 in name, description, runtime and resync_delay_s).

C40. D2: the trade hook, with no consumer

  • What I built. StrategyDirectedRouter.on_trade(venue, trade, now_ns) beside on_book_update; both now share one delivery helper (_deliver_resting_fills, the old body of on_book_update, behaviour unchanged). OMS.on_trade and TradingSession.on_trade(trade) pass it on. A venue adapter without an on_trade method ignores it, so every existing adapter and every result is unchanged.
  • Design point. The core already has a separate canonical TradeEvent; TradingSession.on_event takes MarketEvent only, so trades get their own entry point and never pass through the book-event path. Because of that, a time-integrity-blocked book event cannot swallow a trade: a trade reaches the venue simulator whatever the book event before it did (the venue matches whatever our clock says). The trade does not feed the book gate, the time monitor or the strategy.
  • Tests (test_execution_realism.py, class TradeHookTests, 3 tests): the deterministic adapter and a venue with no adapter ignore a print; an adapter with the hook fills through the same delivery as a book update and the report is stamped with the match time; the OMS passes the trade on; the session hands the trade to the hook and leaves the event counter and the feed-seen times untouched.
  • Identity, March 03-11, solo, after D2: same five profiles, same result: ALL_IDENTICAL for legacy_v0, v1, v1.1; the ft and g1l cells byte-identical (compare_b1_d2_m.log, compare_ledger_c2_d2.log).

C41. D3: QueueAwareVenueAdapter

  • What I built. One new file, crypto_stat_arb/simulation/queue_adapter.py (the name is in your target layout), and its test file tests/test_queue_adapter.py (9 tests). The adapter extends DeterministicVenueAdapter and wraps MakerExecutor (queue v2). A post-only GTC limit order is placed in the executor and acknowledged; a cancel or a replace of such an order goes to the executor (a replace loses the queue place, like an amend on the venue); every other request (IOC, FOK, market) keeps the deterministic walk, which stays for taker fills only (F9). A resting GTC order that is not post-only is refused, so the deterministic adapter's book-cross resting fill can never carry the arb maker leg. The float and Decimal conversion lives in this file and nowhere else.
  • Fill rule. The adapter refuses (ValueError) an executor whose fill_evidence is not a trade-confirm mode, so a depth change can move the queue but never fills an order on its own; with the evidence rule on, the executor fills only on a real print or a cross that a print confirms. The fill reports carry liquidity MAKER, a running cumulative that equals the order's quantity exactly on the last fill, and the cause of every fill is counted in fills_by_source (for the D5 manifest).
  • Tests. Refused executor; a post-only order rests behind the displayed queue (50 ahead), a print of 30 drains it and fills nothing, the next print fills exactly 10 at the limit; a partial then a full fill with the running total; a level that vanishes with no print moves nothing and fills nothing; a post-only price that would cross is rejected and never rests; a non-post-only resting order is refused and an IOC order gives the same reports as the deterministic adapter; cancel then a print fills nothing, a second cancel is refused; replace loses the queue place; the router calls the hook and the report carries the match time.
  • Known difference, for the D5 parity manifest. A core book update does not say whether it was a snapshot or a delta, so the snapshot-shrink signal (on_snapshot) is not fed to the executor; queue movement comes from the diff of the levels our orders rest on (on_delta). Whether this changes fills is measured in D5, not assumed. The queue models that the executor's cohort mode refuses for amend are unchanged.
  • Not wired. Nothing builds this adapter yet, so no replay can be affected; it is proved by its own tests only.

S01: fills maker buy 2.0 @100 and sell 2.0 @99.0, +2.0 / −2.0, orders 2, hedges 1, one signal fully hedged, fees 0.089, cash −199.99 / +197.901: the core matches. S02: 1.2 filled, hedge for 1.2 only, fees 0.0534, cash −119.994 / +118.7406, the remaining 0.8 cancelled when its lifetime ends, still exactly one hedge: matches. S03: no hedge, no position, fees 0, cash 0 / 0, the order cancelled: matches. S03b (depth-only fills from the legacy fill setting) is the maker simulator's behaviour, not a hedge rule.
  • 2a. S06 to S11 and S13 to S15, which phase and why. S06 cancel race and S07 PostOnly reject at arrival: the maker's queue and cancel timing live in the queue simulator, which the core runs only behind the simulated venue adapter (Phase D); the hedge consequence of a cancel race is now a core-path scenario (U5 below). S08 funding across a settlement: the core funding engine, done in B2 (C23, C27, C30). S09 risk limit refuses a signal: the pre-trade check in the core, covered by the skipped-check mutation test and the existing risk tests; the multi-package cap is item 2d. S10 and S10b breaker trip and flatten on trip: the breaker and flatten are Phase E. S11 margin stress to liquidation: core margin engine, B2 step 6 (C24, C27). S13 trade and depth order independence: the maker simulator, Phase D. S14 stale books and timers: the Binance feed-stall and non-valid-book scenarios here. S12 and S15 restart: already run on the core (restart scenarios in test_integration_scenarios.py), plus the restart test of C34.
  • 2b. The five reports. Read: hedge_failure_study_report (summary, section 2, every scenario table), hedge_failure_deep_dive_report (ghost and UNKNOWN, state machine R1 to R4, OMS residual lifecycle and the four failure scenarios), lifecycle_realism_report (rejects, cancel races, the disagreement table D-01 to D-06), perp_lifecycle_report (the conditional scenarios and fault presets), perp_mechanics_report (the fault scenarios before and after). Not read line by line: the latency and acknowledgement calibration tables, the per-coin statistics, the break-even model, the fill-evidence and WP8 to WP10 statistics sections (no failure cases in them). Cases already in H01 to H40 or S1 to S19: every named fault scenario of the reports (rejected, 30% rejected, late 1/5/30 s, outage 2 s/30 s/5 min/120 s on either venue, queue, ghost, blackhole, fully dark, partial hedge, failed unwind, lost ack on hedge and on every order, restart, fast-move rejects, the six breaker triggers, PostOnly rejects, cancel races, D-01 to D-06). Not covered, six cases, each now a core-path scenario (class FailureCasesFromTheReportsNotInH01ToH40OrS1ToS19): U1 an unwind that itself fills only partly (study section 2: a partial unwind is never completed): the core sends the remainder, on the next venue in its priority list, and the pair ends flat net (not flat per venue; the backtester unwinds on the maker venue only). U2 an unwind needed while its venue's book is not valid (study: silent skips): recorded as UNWIND_VENUE_SKIPPED, done on the next venue, nothing silent. U3 a new package while an earlier hedge is UNKNOWN (deep dive: double exposure up to 2x): the core admitted it. That was a gap and it is fixed: while any hedge chain is UNKNOWN (or escalated and waiting on one) no new package is admitted (NEW_RISK_HELD_HEDGE_UNKNOWN, rejected as hedge_unknown_block); after the venue answers a new package is admitted again. U4 every unwind lost (deep dive: stuck unwind, O30 and O120): the three attempts are used, the chain is FAILED with the persistent alarm, no new risk, and the kill switch trips 30 s into the breach (at 35.5 s for a breach that began at 5.0 s). U5 the cancel loses the race (lifecycle report: cancel found nothing): the venue answers the cancel with order-does-not-exist, the order stays CANCEL_PENDING, the late fill arrives and is hedged; both chains reconcile, +2.0 / −2.0. U6 a reject report after a maker fill (D-06): the fill is kept, hedged, one alarm and one re-query.
  • 2c. Fee parity. The core instrument fee rates are set to the old path's (bybit maker −0.5 bps, taker 5.5; binance maker 2.0, taker 5.0) and every core-path scenario now compares cash per venue and fees as well as fills and positions, to nine decimals: S01, S02, S03, S04, S05 (hand-calculated as S02), S16 a and b, S17, S18, S19 all equal the old path's numbers. The comparison found two real defects, both fixed: (1) the maker child was not marked post-only and GTC, so it was priced as a TAKER (fee 0 on the maker leg in the test; a −0.5 bps rebate expected) and read as an unknown IOC by the deadline logic; it is now post-only GTC, priced as maker and never queried while it rests quietly (test added). (2) A maker fill arriving as a stream update (the normal FILL_TRIGGERED input) was never fee-priced, because the OMS priced fills only on dispatch callbacks and reconciliation; under FILL_TRIGGERED the reducer now prices each new fill delta once.
  • 2d. Concurrent chains are capped by the package reservation. ConcurrentChainsAreCappedByThePackageReservation: five hedges accepted and left in flight, maker fills of 0.002, 0.005, 0.009, 0.010 give four chains in flight at once (0.002, 0.003, 0.004, 0.001); after every fill the total hedge quantity (live and filled) never exceeds the 0.01 the one package reservation holds for the hedge leg, there is exactly one reservation throughout (never topped up) and the held quantity does not change. A venue that over-reports the maker (cumulative 0.012 of 0.01) is refused by the OMS reducer (invalid cumulative fill) and, past the reducer, the hedge would still be bounded by the declared maximum. A second test mixes filled, resting, rejected and retried hedges: the chains together cover exactly the maker size and the filled or resting hedges stay within the reservation.
  • 3. Owner decisions of 8 Nov, each labelled DECISION_PENDING_CALIBRATION in the config (oms.residual.decision_status and decision_notes in all six core config copies; the config file is a release artifact, so the labels are part of the release identity through its hash) and in the working profile v1_4's decision_status (the backtester's release identity): unwind band 15 bps (the core's code default and its config copies were 10; the backtester profile already used 15; the core's unwind price band changes from 10 to 15 bps, which changes the core's behaviour); residual limit = the core's two stages, hold new risk at 5 s (max_residual_age_ms) and kill switch at 30 s (kill_after_ms); unwind attempt budget 3: it was 2 in the core repo's config copies (src/crypto_stat_arb/config/research.json, perp_research.json, paper_watch.json; the code default and the top-level config copies were already 3), changed to 3 on 8 Nov, which changes live behaviour: one more unwind attempt before the alarm; this is written in decision_notes and pinned by test; H16 = FAILED with alarm and kill switch, accepted. The new files hedge_lifecycle.py and the three test files are accepted.
  • Tests. test_hedge_lifecycle.py 149, test_fill_triggered.py 34, test_core_hedge_scenarios.py 18, plus the touched files (test_oms.py, test_perp_venue_semantics.py, test_requote.py, test_oms_order_tracking.py, test_core_accounting.py, test_wp7_profiles.py, test_r12_breaker.py, and the OMS, execution, restart and residual tests): all pass; 584 tests selected by name (residual, unwind, config, oms, hedge, release, execution) passed before the last additions.
  • March 2024-03-11 identity (2 h) after the review changes, run solo: legacy_v0, v1 and v1.1 against b1: trades, fill events, bucket stats, model settings and every summary field identical (compare_b1_c2_m.log, ALL_IDENTICAL true); v1_2 (cell ft) and v1_4 (cell g1l) identical to the previous Phase C identity cells in the four files and every summary field. Wrappers baselines/after_c1.sh and after_c2.sh.

The foreign strategy_mm/ folder is untouched and unstaged.
  • Left for you: where strategy_mm/ lives; the band and the residual limit are now your recorded decisions but stay labelled DECISION_PENDING_CALIBRATION until measured; S01 to S03 are core-path comparisons with synthetic books (wiring, not results).

Phase C (8 Nov 2026): core fill-triggered hedging

Built in the core repo (crypto_stat_arb) and the outer repo's tests. Everything numeric is EXPLORATORY; the scenarios are synthetic (wiring and rules, no profit figure). The backtester keeps its own hedging until Phase D, so v1.1, v1_2 and v1_4 replays are unchanged: checked below.

C33. What was built

  • Hedge lifecycle state machine (crypto_stat_arb/hedge_lifecycle.py, new, named by your specification). Twelve states (MAKER_FILLED, HEDGE_PENDING, SUBMITTED, PARTIALLY_FILLED, HEDGED, REJECTED, RETRYING, UNKNOWN, UNWINDING, ESCALATED, RECONCILED, FAILED), one transition table, move() raises on any other move and on a move that lacks its evidence: RETRYING needs a classified reject that permits it (or a venue query that did not find the order) and budget left; leaving UNKNOWN needs a query result that says so (an unacknowledged order is never assumed failed); FAILED from UNKNOWN only after the query budget; RECONCILED with an open residual only by the operator reset from FAILED. Every refused move changes nothing (validate, then mutate). It records per event: retry intervals (failure known to retry sent), reject labels, timeouts, partial fills, the outcome and the residual-exposure duration; it snapshots and restores for a restart. PLAN_TO_HEDGE and INTENT_TO_HEDGE map PlanState and IntentState onto it; the mapping is pinned by test (every intent transition has a path in the table).
  • One reject-code table in the core (crypto_stat_arb/venue_semantics_perp.py, moved, rows unchanged; new_backtest/venue_semantics_perp.py is a re-export until Phase F). New: retry_permitted on every reject row (only a rate limit is retryable; margin, minimum, reduce-only, position mode, PostOnly and a generic reject are not) and classify_reject, the one entry point. Engine-side refusals with no venue code (ioc_unfilled, price_cap_exceeded: retryable; expired_at_release: not) live in a separate small table so mapping_table() stays observed or spec only. An unclassified reason is not retryable (fail closed) and is counted. OMS._classify_reject runs it once for every rejected child and records label, retry answer and basis.
  • FILL_TRIGGERED (DispatchPolicy.FILL_TRIGGERED, OMS.execute(..., maker_venue=)). The package (maker and hedge, worst case) is reserved atomically; only the post-only GTC maker is sent; the hedge child does not exist until a maker fill. DETERMINISTIC_SEQUENTIAL is untouched (the per-leg body of the package loop became _dispatch_leg, shared by both, and the whole OMS suite is green). Rules, all in the OMS: second partial fill while a hedge is in flight: every maker fill delta opens its own chain, one live child per chain, nothing queued and nothing netted, the reservation bounds the sum at the maker size (this is the backtester's rule, written down: one hedge intent per fill); idempotent client order id: the hedge child id is a stable id of parent, chain and attempt, recorded (HEDGE_SUBMIT.client_order_id) and persisted before the send, so a redelivered report or a restart cannot create a second hedge; unknown block: a silent hedge is never resent, the OMS queries the venue at the report deadline and the chain waits in UNKNOWN until the query answers; reject: classified, a retryable one is retried after the backoff within HedgePolicy.retry_limit (default 0: retry is off until a caller sets it, no production value invented), anything else goes to the unwind path; an IOC remainder retries only the unfilled quantity; unwind uses the existing residual machinery (bound, attempts, alarm); recovery: the declared hedge leg and every chain are persisted and rebuilt from the plan store on restart.
  • Hedge on a non-valid book (finding 7), the core rule. A hedge whose book is missing, not VALID, one-sided or older than HedgePolicy.max_book_age_ns is HELD in HEDGE_PENDING, re-checked on every timer and book update, sent when the book is valid, and escalated by the residual age limit if it never is. It is never sent blind, never dropped, and never flattened at once (the backtester's refuse-and-flatten, and its fault path that skips the book state, both go away here). A price beyond the cap is not a refusal: the hedge reduces exposure just taken, so it is sent at its cap and the breach is recorded; an IOC that finds nothing is the retryable ioc_unfilled.
  • H16 settled. When status queries keep failing the chain goes to FAILED (persistent alarm, no new risk until an operator reset) and the core's kill switch trips and resting makers are cancelled. Both, not one: the exposure is of unknown size, so it can be neither hedged nor unwound blind, and the resting maker must stop adding to it. The backtester raises only the alarm; that stays until Phase D.
  • One setting each, DECISION_PENDING_CALIBRATION (OMS.decision_pending_settings(), printed in hedge_report() beside the backtester's value): unwind band 10 bps core against 15 bps backtester; unwind attempt budget: the core's code default was already 3 (2 Nov); the 2 you saw is the core repo's own config copies (research.json, perp_research.json, paper_watch.json under crypto_stat_arb/config), now set to 3 so the copies agree (pinned by test); residual limit: core age 5 s then hold new risk, kill after 30 s (config), backtester a 5 s net-exposure breaker that trips at once. I settled the budget; the band and the residual limit are left as two labelled values because choosing between 10 and 15 bps or between hold-then-kill and trip-at-once is your decision (they are the owner's DECISION_PENDING_CALIBRATION items).
  • EMS01 (ReleasePolicy, ThrottleAction.EXPIRED, StrategyDirectedRouter.release_revalidator, OMS._revalidate_queued). Every queued SUBMIT is re-checked when it is released, against the market as it is then, for every queued route: kill switch on, book not valid, queue age over max_queue_age_ns (None: no limit, none invented), or the touch moved past an IOC's limit expire it. An expired request is not sent: it is rejected as expired_at_release (not retryable), counted and traced (THROTTLE_EXPIRED). A risk-reducing request is exempt (it closes exposure). On by default; the existing OMS suite is unchanged.
  • The five unread EMS settings. primary.price_rule is validated against PRICE_RULES (conditional_edge for every perp profile, naive_edge for legacy_v0) and read by the passive tactic. hedge.q, maker_maker.escalate_after_s, unwind.window_s and unwind.fallback were set by no profile: removed (a config that names one is refused). The known-unused list in test_requote.py is now empty and stays empty.
  • Skipped-risk-check mutation. With the pre-trade check intact a package on stale books is refused and nothing is sent; with it mutated away the same package is sent; the test fails if the mutant survives.
  • p_fail and loss_fail_bps are SCENARIO, not calibrated. Relabelled in the working profile perp_perp_maker_taker_v1_4 (decision_status and the comments) and in the switch notes; the frozen and reference profiles keep their files. Failure costs are reported per scenario: hedge_report() carries the label and no rate.

  • H01 to H40 against the table. 28 cases are a legal path through the transition table, each ending RECONCILED or FAILED and none open (H01, H02, H03, H04, H06, H07, H08, H09, H10, H11, H12, H15, H16, H18, H19, H20, H21, H22, H24, H26, H27, H29, H30, H31, H32, H34, H36, H37); 12 are outside the hedge chain and say who owns them (H05 oracle, H13 and H14 entry gates, H17 maker order lifecycle, H23 and H25 Phase E, H28 maker PostOnly, H33 pre-trade risk, H35 ledger, H38 margin, H39 Phase E, H40 stated limit). The two things the core must not do (carry a failed hedge; retry an unknown order with no query) are refused by the table.
  • Phase 0 scenarios through the core path (test_core_hedge_scenarios.py; fills, positions and counts compared with the backtester's hand-calculated result; cash and fees are not compared, the core prices fees with its own schedule, a Phase D comparison): S04 (retry twice then unwind: same fills, 2 retries, 3 rejected attempts, 1 unwind, flat); S05 (the pinned expected failure on the backtester: the core passes it: one hedge ever exists, no unwind, no ghost over-hedge, bybit +1.2 / binance −1.2, the 1.2 stays reserved until the query finds it); S16 a and b (late hedge fills; hedge finds nothing inside its limit then unwind: same fills and positions, open 1.003 s); S17 (partial hedge: same three fills, +1.0 / −1.0); S18 (the pinned expected failure: the core passes it: no unwind before the query, the unwind after the query finds nothing); S19 (lost ack resolved by query, no retry); the non-valid book (held, then sent, or escalated; the backtester's old result was refuse-and-flatten at once).
  • Not run through the core path, and why. S01, S02, S03, S06, S07, S13 are maker-queue and market-data scenarios: the queue simulator runs in the core only behind the simulated adapter, which is Phase D. S08 funding and S11 margin are the B2 core engines (C23, C24, C27, C30). S09 is the pre-trade risk check (covered by the mutation test and the existing risk tests). S10 and S10b are the breaker, Phase E. S12 and S15 already run on the core (restart scenarios in test_integration_scenarios.py). S14 is covered by the feed-stall and non-valid-book tests. The Binance feed stall (no update for 1.6 s just after the Bybit fill): with no age limit the hedge goes on the last valid book at once; with a limit below the stall it waits for the next update; a stall past the residual age limit ends in the escalation and nothing is sent on a book it could not trust. The limit is a labelled setting, not a default.

C31. Hedge execution and recovery audit (8 Nov; report only, no code changed)

Read from the live tree on 8 Nov: new_backtest/backtester.py, hedge_faults.py, circuit_breaker.py, venue_semantics_perp.py, profile perp_perp_maker_taker_v1_2.yaml, core oms.py, execution.py, resilience.py, ems/reconcile.py, config.py, and the catalogue tests/integration_hedge_failure_catalogue.json (H01 to H40). Nothing was run for this audit except one platform_kit test to read its failure. Your seven preliminary findings are checked one by one; two are corrected.

Reading it. The gap is exactly 2.000 ms on every record I read, for both venues, trades, book events and snapshots, on all eleven days: p50 = p99 = min = max (the extra 0.00009 ms is float rounding). A real receive stamp has jitter; a constant offset with no spread is not a measurement of a network. So the March local_ts was constructed as exchange time + 2 ms, and the data cannot tell where it was recorded: nothing about Tokyo, Singapore or any host can be read from it. Consequences: (1) the replay thinks both venues' market data arrives 2 ms after the exchange stamp, which is the tokyo preset's Binance feed (2 ms) and not the singapore preset's 36 ms, so singapore runs on March data are optimistic about how fresh the Binance book is when the strategy acts; (2) because every record carries a local stamp, the presets' feed delays are not applied to March data at all; (3) any feed-latency figure for this data must come from somewhere else (the Binance PDF measures Binance only, and a recording host is not named). Also: the clock offset between host and exchange is ASSUMED 0 and cannot be read from this data. I did not look at January: the timestamps-going-backwards counts in B3 show that January local_ts are real stamps.
  1. 130 ms sensitivity case (hedge move −0.862, file config/hedge_move_arrival130ms_mar2024_train_0301_0307_v1.json, sha256 6d99fccd…ba83; run through the new runner flag --hedge-move-sensitivity, labelled SENSITIVITY in the summary; the profile file is not edited): see the last row of the table in B8. With the smaller edge room the strategy signals 4 times instead of 7 and keeps 2 fills.

B2. Message-atomic book (v1_2 switch message_atomic_book; off in v1 and v1.1)

  • Rule. An exchange message is the run of consecutive book deltas of one venue with the same exchange time and the same recorded receive stamp; a trade, a snapshot or a new exchange time ends it. With the switch on, the book applies the whole message before anything else runs: a crossed state between two deltas is neither repaired nor counted; a cross still there at the message end is counted once and repaired as before. The strategy, the touch, the marks, the venue state and the maker queue updates run after the whole message, in arrival order. Code: OrderBook.message_atomic and end_message() (core), _mark_frames and _delta_in_message (backtester). 8 new tests in tests/test_message_atomic_book.py.
  • The message boundary, from the data. In the Bybit March files the deltas group into runs of identical exchange time that come every 100 ms (gap between groups p1 97 ms, p50 100 ms, p99 104 ms, max 300 ms on 03-08 and 03-11; about 71,900 groups per 2-hour slice, median 35 to 73 deltas each, up to 1,761). Per delta the engine counted 5,656 to 12,133 crossings per slice; at group ends there are none in all four slices (B3 table). The crossings were all inside groups. This is evidence that the 100 ms group is the unit the book is consistent in; it is not proof of the exchange's wire message, which these files do not carry (no sequence ids). The earlier B0.1 check also agrees (the rebuilt book matches the snapshots between groups).
  • Two mistakes, found by looking at the numbers. (1) The first runs showed identical results with the switch on: the feed spaces receive stamps by one float step per record (about 0.24 microseconds at today's epoch), so my "same receive stamp" test never matched and no message was ever grouped. (2) The second version grouped messages but a 400-delta message spans 0.1 ms, so the other venue's events could fall inside it and cut it in two (the engine then still counted 57 to 280 crossings per slice, all of them cuts of a message). Now other-venue events inside a message's span are applied just after it (counted: 81 per 400,000 events on 03-08) and the book counts 0 crossings on the March slices, the same as the scan. Both flawed sets of runs are kept under the names above and are not used.

Effect on signals and fills, one change at a time (v1_2, four SELECT slices; "strategy evaluations" = how many times the strategy looked at the books):

B3. Data checks, items 1 to 8 (observation only: no gate, replay or result used for a byte comparison reads them)

  1. One rulebook. quality.py stays the rulebook and quality_preflight.py its counter. The new scan (scan_venue_day_checks, data_checks_report) uses the defect names of QualitySnapshot and the engine's own OrderBook for the cross rule. Limits differ on purpose. Preflight limits are per day (counts over a day): max_stale_intervals 30, max_crossed_books 0, max_out_of_order 0, max_price_jumps 3, max_wide_spreads 5, max_invalid_levels 0, max_invalid_trades 0, min_events 100 (the full set is in each report file). Inline rules judge one moment: book older than max_book_age_s 0.5 s is stale, feed silent beyond dead_feed_s 5 s is dead, cross-venue skew beyond 0.2 s blocks entry. The preflight stale test is 30 s, the engine's dead-feed limit is 5 s; neither was changed.
  2. MEASURED or NOT_MEASURED for every defect type. In each preflight report (out/integration/phase_b/data_checks/*.json, key defects): MEASURED are stale intervals, crossed books (at snapshots), price jumps, wide spreads, invalid levels, silent feeds, out of order and invalid trades (the last two are new). NOT_MEASURED, with a reason and a count of null, never 0: sequence gaps, book-level duplicates, negative latency, impossible latency, schema exceptions, rejected records, rejected partitions, unknown instrument rules, persistence lag, unpersisted bytes. The existing data_quality block in run results is unchanged. Test: tests/test_data_checks.py asserts that no unmeasured defect shows a count.
  3. Added to the preflight: out-of-order timestamps (a record whose exchange or receive time is below the previous record's, in the loader's merged order) and a scan of the trade files for invalid trades (price or size not finite and positive, empty side). Sequence gaps stay NOT_MEASURED: the files carry no sequence ids in any of the datasets I scanned: January 2022 (Bybit and Binance) and March 2024 (Bybit and Binance); the replay checks ids inline when a feed has them (apply_sequence), which none of these do. Added scan time is in the last column of the table below (seconds of the existing preflight scan against the new scan, which does more: it keeps a full book, reads trades and checks every record; they ran with other jobs on the machine, so read the ratio, about 0.9 to 1.6 times the existing scan, not the seconds).
  4. Stale gaps at 5 s and 30 s, seconds lost (between consecutive book events; limits unchanged): see the two gap columns.   • Out-of-order timestamps are a January problem: Bybit 01-13 has 104,891 records with an exchange time below the previous record's and 19,889 on the receive clock; Binance January has none on the exchange clock but 76,543 and 136,554 on the receive clock. March has none. The merge in the loader does not repair these (it merges already-sorted files); the replay forces receive times to increase by a float step. I did not measure what they do to January results.
  • No invalid trades, no negative sizes, no snapshot crosses on any venue-day. Crossed at a message end: Bybit January 122 (01-05) and 9 (01-13), everything else 0.
  1. Does the hedge path with faults check the book state? No. The taker-arrival path checks book.status() != "valid" when enforce_book_state is on and misses with book_not_valid. With the fault layer on, a hedge returns to _execute_hedge_with_faults before that check, which has no state check. Pinned in HedgeOnANonValidBook (tests): hedge venue book 5 s old at arrival. Without faults: the hedge is refused (book_not_valid) and the legacy instant unwind flattens the maker fill on Bybit at once (nothing waited, nothing retried). With faults: the hedge executes on the stale book. The rule for a hedge refused on a bad book (wait, retry or unwind) goes in Phase C with a scenario. Nothing was changed here.
  2. Side, unit and tick checks at data arrival (core dataset_manifest.py, new class ArrivalChecks; no new manifest file; observation only):

  • Sides: books use bid/ask everywhere; trades use b/s in January 2022 and buy/sell in March 2024 (both accepted; the loaders read the first letter). Tick (declared: Bybit 0.005 and Binance 0.001 in January, 0.001 in March): all prices on the grid except 17 Bybit prices off the 0.005 tick on 01-13 (of 17.9 million). Size valid: no violations. Lot step: NOT_MEASURED (no lot step for SOLUSDT is stored; lot and minimum checks need the instrument master, REF03 and REF04); at 1 SOL clips they would not bind, but that is a statement, not a check.
  1. The run stops when quality.py cannot load, override or not. Before: with allow_rejected_data set (every headline run sets it) the feed ran the preflight in report-only mode, and an unavailable quality.py returned "skipped" and the run went on unchecked. Now the feed asks for the rulebook (require_rules) and raises. Tests: RulebookMustLoad (the preflight raises with the override on; the feed raises when the replay starts). Headline runs that use a preflight override: all of them: the January baselines (reason: Binance trade tape truncated at about 17 h on 01-05 and 01-13), every March slice and probe (reason: 2-hour slice, Binance March L1 only, manifest EMPTY). In none of them did the preflight need it: the preflight health was HEALTHY and APPROVED for both venues with no rejected venue, blocking: false only because the flag is set. The same flag serves the data-manifest gate and the preflight; I suggest separating them (a justification for the manifest should not also switch the quality gate to report-only). Not done.

All twelve venue-day reports are in out/integration/phase_b/data_checks/; the full-day March Bybit scans (TRAIN days 03-01, 03-04, 03-07) are in B3b.

B3b. Crossed-book measurement beyond the slices

Three full TRAIN days of Bybit March 2024 (24 hours each; HOLDOUT untouched; Binance March has no book events, so only Bybit is scanned): 03-01: 75,179,149 deltas in 862,315 messages (860,092 with more than one delta), 0 crossed at message end, 0 at snapshots, 0 / 0 timestamps backwards, 0 negative sizes, 0 invalid trades, 0 gaps above 5 s; 03-04: 78,915,481 deltas in 862,137 messages (859,549 with more than one delta), 0 crossed at message end, 0 at snapshots, 0 / 0 timestamps backwards, 0 negative sizes, 0 invalid trades, 0 gaps above 5 s; 03-07: 102,029,780 deltas in 863,023 messages (861,962 with more than one delta), 0 crossed at message end, 0 at snapshots, 0 / 0 timestamps backwards, 0 negative sizes, 0 invalid trades, 0 gaps above 5 s. About 864,000 messages per day is exactly one every 100 ms. The March Bybit result holds across 3 full days and 4 two-hour slices: no cross at a message end. January is different (Bybit 122 and 9 at message ends). The 100 ms spacing and the zero count together are the evidence for the message unit; the scans took 13 to 27 minutes per day.

B4. Accounting-only shadow (new_backtest/core_shadow.py, the one approved new file; ArbBacktestConfig.core_shadow, off by default)

  • Every fill the backtester books is also given to the core Ledger (as a core ChildOrder) and priced with the core FeeSchedule. The shadow only reads; a run with it on gave results identical to the run without it (d4 against d3: every field). Compared: position per venue after every fill, cash per venue, fees (backtester fee against the core rate at the same notional), and ending equity (common-timestamp against each venue's own last mid). Not compared: funding, margin and liquidation, the core's risk and OMS. Tolerances 1e-9 base and 1e-6 USDT. Tests: tests/test_core_shadow.py (agreement, wrong fee, dropped fill, missing fee rule, off by default, config against presets).
  • Fee rates. The core config perp_research.json carried Bybit maker +2.0 bps for BTC. I added SOL rows (Bybit maker −0.5 / taker 5.5; Binance 2.0 / 5.0, all fee_source ASSUMED) so the core FeeSchedule and the backtester's headline presets give the same rates for SOLUSDT (tested). The BTC rows are unchanged and act as the conservative scenario. Two copies of this file exist (platform config/ and the core package config/, differing in max_unwind_attempts 3 against 2); I edited the core one. They do the same job: please say which stays.
March slice (v1_2, shadow on)Fills shadowedPositions agreeFees: backtester / core (USDT)Trade-cash gap after the fee differenceEquity gap not explained by fees (USDT)Differences, classified
03-080yes0 / 00.00e+00+0.0000none

03-112yes0.0632765 / 0.0632765-4.00e-14+0.0080A2_VALUATION (BY_DESIGN)
  • Differences, sorted. Positions, cash and fees agree on every slice and fill. The one difference is equity on 03-11: A2, BY_DESIGN (the backtester marks both venues at the last time both had data, the core marks each at its own last mid; 0.008 USDT = 1 SOL times the mid gap between the two venues' last updates). Under matrix A2 it is BACKTEST_AHEAD: the core should adopt the common timestamp (not done, B10). January 01-13 v1.1 shadow (run through the same runner, outputs byte-identical to the unshadowed run): fills 42, positions agree: True, fees backtester 1.5536168 / core 1.5536168, differences: A2_VALUATION (BY_DESIGN). Two things in the January run. (1) The backtester booked funding into cash on that day (+0.000436 USDT Bybit, −0.000444 Binance, net −0.0000088); the core funding engine is not in the shadow, so the shadow takes funding out of the backtester's side and reports it; after that trade cash agrees to 5e-14 USDT (float rounding), positions agree (−1.25 and +1.25 SOL residual) and fees agree to 1e-18. (My first January shadow run showed this as an unclassified trade-cash gap; funding explained it and the shadow now handles it.) (2) The A2 difference is 6.45 USDT on a day whose result is −0.70 USDT. The 1.25 SOL residual sits on two venues whose last updates are 24,308 s (6.75 hours) apart on 01-13 (venue_last_update_spread_s in the run's equity bridge; the backtester's own flag lagging_venue_flag is true): valuing each venue at its own last mid, as the core does, gives an ending equity of +5.75 USDT against the common-timestamp −0.70, a phantom gain of 6.45 USDT, about nine times the day's loss. That is the same mechanism as the +17 USDT phantom the R11 valuation fix removed on 2022-01-05. Priority for the port of common-timestamp valuation into the core (not done, B10). No BUG_BACKTEST or BUG_CORE found; the C8 float-against-Decimal tolerance was never hit.
  • Mutation checks (tests): a wrong fee is reported as C1_FEE_RATE (BY_DESIGN), a dropped fill as a position mismatch, a missing core fee rule as BUG_CORE. The funding-sign mutation cannot be run (funding is not in the shadow).
  • Decimal at the simulator boundary. Measured: converting price and size to Decimal costs about 1.5 microseconds per delta against 0.24 for the float multiply it replaces; a 2-hour March slice has 4 to 10 million deltas, so converting every delta would add 6 to 15 s (a few per cent) before any Decimal book arithmetic. The shadow's work per fill (Decimal child order plus core ledger) is about 96 microseconds, and there are tens of fills per day. Decision: floats stay inside the simulator; Decimal only at the fill boundary (shadow, later the ledger); to be recorded in the run manifest when the switch-over is built.

B5. Margin rule A9, query before replace (OMS03): v1_2 switches

  • A9. margin.above_last_tier: ignore (v1, v1.1: a position above the last margin tier has no maintenance figure and is never liquidated) or breach (v1_2, the core rule POSITION_ABOVE_RISK_LIMIT, fail closed: it is liquidated like a margin breach). Tests in test_margin_model.py. At 1 SOL (about 140 USDT) the last tier is never reached, so there is no effect on any slice (the breach counter is not even created).
  • Query before replace. The backtester already queries before replacing for an UNKNOWN acknowledgement (WP6; scenario S19 passes), so the matrix row C6 is wrong for that path. What it did not do is the timeout of a hedge sent into an outage (blackhole or ghost): it wrote the order off at the ack timeout and the retry or unwind was a replacement sent blind (scenarios S05 and S18, pinned as expected failures). hedge_faults.query_before_replace (v1_2 true): the timeout starts a venue status query instead, answered unknown_resolve_s later and not before the gateway is back and a ghost has landed; a hedge the query finds executed is done, one it does not find goes down the normal ladder. Tests S05S18QueryBeforeReplace: ghost, no unwind and net 0 after the query; blackhole, no unwind at 7.5 s and one unwind after the query found nothing. Clean runs never reach this path (d4 equals d3). The S05 and S18 expected-failure tests stay as they are: they run today's default.

B6. Release identity (for the freeze, not frozen)

The identity already carried the profile hash, code hash, data hash, fee snapshot, instrument master hash, calibration file hash and cutoff, the pinned hedge-move file with its hash, and the execution config hash. Added: both repositories' commit and a dirty flag (release_identity.git.outer and .core; tracked files only), tested. Proposed freeze content for v1_2 (needs your approval; nothing is frozen): rounding switch, probe hedge move (now the 7-day 45 ms arrival estimate), message-atomic book, query before replace, A9, the 35 ms floor, the join switch. Query before replace was built as above; "gap handling" is in the engine already (apply_sequence) but no dataset here has ids, so it is not exercised.

B7. Dataset settings (proposal, nothing built)

v1_2 carries dataset facts: venue_ticks, maker_venues, the hedge-move file, the floors for the singapore preset. Proposal: split the profile into a strategy block (edge, rounding rule, switches, hedge-fault policy, margin rule: one version, any dataset) and a dataset block (ticks, lot step, maker venue, hedge-move file with its TRAIN days, latency floors), with ticks and lots read from the instrument master once REF03 provides them; the dataset block is what a run's identity hashes separately, so a January or a new-symbol run changes the dataset block and not the strategy version.

Separate full replays of the four slices, v1_2 final (d4), only the named setting changed. The headline stays proportional; the range sits beside it.

  • All four queue models give identical signals, orders, fills, trades and P&L on these slices. Under v1.1's evidence rules a depth decrease alone cannot fill an order, so how the queue ahead is depleted no longer decides a fill (the same result the ladder report found on 2022-01-05 for the first three). The cohort-uniform queue runs with opposite_cross_fills, its confirm window and the cap-to-print off (it refuses them), and still gives the same two fills. The range across the four models is zero on four 2-hour slices with two fills: that says the model choice did not decide those fills, not that the models agree in general. Evidence-suppressed fills (the diagnostics the model choice does change) are in the run summaries.
  • The 130 ms case (−0.862) changes the strategy (4 signals instead of 7) because the required edge is larger; it keeps 2 fills, and its 1 s maker markout is −1.92 bps against −0.03.

B10. Not done, not proven, decisions for you

  • Not done (named in the Phase B brief): (1) switching the backtester to the core FeeSchedule, funding, margin and ledger and retiring funding_settler, margin_model and the fee presets. The shadow shows positions, cash and fees agree today at the headline rates, which is the precondition; the switch itself needs the Decimal boundary decision (float cash into a Decimal ledger without changing a byte of v1.1 output) and a funding and margin shadow first. (2) Porting common-timestamp valuation into the core (A2). (3) Lot and minimum checks (no SOLUSDT rules stored). (4) Funding and margin in the shadow (March has no funding; January funding settles in the backtester only). (5) The freeze itself (needs your approval).
  • Not proven: that the 100 ms Bybit group is the exchange's wire message (no ids in the files); any effect size of half-applied messages (two to twenty-four fills); that January's out-of-order timestamps are harmless (not measured); that the March Binance feed is as fresh as the replay assumes (the 2 ms gap is constructed).
  • Decisions for you: (a) the March timestamp finding: should the singapore preset keep running on data whose receive stamps are constructed at 2 ms, or do you want a labelled run with an assumed Binance feed delay? (b) separate the manifest override from the preflight override (B3, item 8); (c) which copy of perp_research.json stays; (d) approve the v1_2 freeze content (B6) or change it; (e) the switch-over of fees, funding, margin and ledger: go ahead in Phase B, or move to a Phase B2 with its own gate.

MakerExecutor.obs_enabled is now a property; turning observations on installs the counting wrappers on that executor, turning them off removes them.
