F3. Hedge arrival from the fill, broken into its parts (latency evidence (a))

The engine's hedge timing was read from backtester.py (_on_fill) and timing.py, then checked against the one real fill with a hedge on 2024-03-11 and against the same numbers recomputed from the seeded streams. The sum below reproduces the logged hedge time exactly (31.46 ms singapore, 44.4 ms tokyo).

StepWhat the engine doesSingapore, first hedge of a runTokyo, first hedge of a runModel mean (singapore)
0. The fill is stampedthe maker fill takes the local stamp of the print that fills it. The print's exchange time is 2.0 ms earlier (this 2 ms is the gap in the data, not a preset value)t = 0t = 0n/a
1. Private reportBybit fill_report timeline: the strategy learns of the fill one report latency after the print's exchange time, and not before the stamped fill (a maximum of the two). The 2.0 ms already elapsed in the data is credited against itdraw 3.09 ms from the exchange time, so +1.09 ms after the stamped filldraw 43.15 ms, so +41.15 ms2.0 ms (preset Bybit feed), about 0.7 ms after the 2 ms credit
2. DecisionNo separate stage. The hedge is dispatched at the report time. The strategy's own compute time is not simulated0 ms0 ms0 ms
3. EntryBinance entry timeline: dispatch to arrival at the matching engine30.37 ms3.30 ms40.0 ms (preset Binance entry)
Hedge arrival from the stamped fill31.46 ms (logged 31.46)44.45 ms (logged 44.4)42.0 ms by the code sum; 40.7 ms after the data credit

Why 31 ms is below “about 45 ms”.

  1. I cannot get 45 from the preset. The singapore preset gives Bybit feed 2.0 plus Binance entry 40.0 = 42.0 ms as the sum of means, and the engine has no decision stage that would add the missing ~3 ms. (Tokyo also sums to 42: Bybit feed 36 plus Binance entry 6.) If you want a decision or dispatch time in the model, that is a model change I have not made.
  2. 31.46 ms is one draw, not a distribution. The timeline streams are seeded (seed 11) and restart at every run, and the hedge uses the first draw of the Binance entry stream and of the Bybit report stream. The first singapore draws are 30.37 ms and 3.09 ms. That is why 03-09 and 03-11 both show 31.5 ms: they are the same draw twice, not two observations.
  3. The draw is a low one. The Binance entry draw 30.37 ms is low: about 13 % of entry draws (mean 40, std 8) are at or below 31.5 ms. Simulating the two lognormal streams with the 2 ms data credit gives a mean of 40.7 ms, median 39.9, 10th to 90th percentile 30.9 to 51.5 ms; 31.46 ms or lower happens in about 12 % of draws.
  4. The data's own feed gap is credited against the report. That removes about 1.3 ms from the mean (42.0 to 40.7).
  5. Other hedges in the same clean v1.1 runs (paired by order of appearance, so retries may be in the mix; 13 delays over 01-05, 01-13 and 03-11): 28.5 to 52.6 ms, median 34.3, mean 37.4. That fits a mean near 40, not 45.

Not measured. These are preset numbers (ASSUMED, host location not stated), not a measurement on mainnet. The replay applies the data's own local stamps to market data; per the timing module, the preset feed delay is used only for records without a local stamp. I did not test that separately.

F4. Hedge-move probe on the 7 TRAIN days, at 1 s and at fixed delays (item 3, latency evidence (b))

Coverage. Every day covers only the 00:00 to 02:00 UTC slice (the existing probe on the existing slice layout; the slices for 03-04 to 03-07 were built with the existing make_slices.py, which reads data/ and writes only under out/queue_v2/slices_mar2024). The probe is the unchanged qv2_probe.py: one 1 SOL Bybit bid per 15 s slot at 6.546 bps, v1.1 fill rules, Binance mid move after each simulated fill. Bid side only. TRAIN days 03-01 to 03-07 only: no SELECT or HOLDOUT day was used. The pinned −0.771 is unchanged. No haircut.

Delay assumption. The windows at 30, 45, 60, 100, 130 and 200 ms are fixed delays from the fill, computed offline from each probe fill and the Binance best bid and ask series on the local clock; no extra replay and no change to the probe's results (the 1 s column is the probe's own output; 03-01 gives the same fills and counts as before). They are set-ups, not measurements. For orientation only: the singapore model gives 31 to 52 ms for a hedge (F3), and tokyo 44 ms for the first hedge. None of this is measured on mainnet. Sign: negative = the Binance mid fell after a bid fill, adverse for the sell hedge.

B0.1 Frame-atomic rebuild check (no strategy run)

Rebuild acceptance on the four January venue-days, run on the live tree after Phase A. A "frame" is the set of messages with the same exchange time and receive time. Each variant is rebuilt from deltas only between two snapshots and compared with the next snapshot. "Touch price" = best bid and best ask price both equal; "size" = price and size both equal; top-10 = all ten levels per side. New in this phase (measurement code in queue_v2/rebuild.py only): two engine variants that apply a frame as one unit and run the engine's crossed-book repair once, after the frame (engine_frame_*). The four older variants reproduced the earlier results (2 Nov) digit for digit.

Venue, dayVariantTouch price %Touch size %Top-10 %Crossed events

Tape repair on gives the same picture (full table in out/integration/b0/rebuild_acceptance/*.json).

Result. Applying a frame as one unit does not change the touch match on any of the four days: the largest difference is 0.001 percentage points (Bybit 01-05, engine book). It does remove the crossed states that sit inside a frame: Binance 16,435 (01-13) and 7,865 (01-05) engine crossings become 0; Bybit 01-13 exact 238 become 27. Why the match cannot move: the comparison always happens between frames, so the per-message and per-frame books are equal at every compare point by construction. This check therefore cannot show the benefit of frame-atomic application; the benefit is in the states a strategy would see between messages, and that is measured in Phase B (signals after a whole message). One oddity: for Bybit 01-05 the engine counts more crossings per frame (122) than per message (92), because the per-message repair removes levels before the frame ends; I did not investigate it further.

B0.2 Hedge move: probe estimate against realised hedges

What was done. The expected hedge move for the bid is now the probe estimate: −0.771 bps (n = 834 fill events, standard error 0.114, TRAIN days 03-01 to 03-03; the old market-data number was −0.654). It is pinned in the profile by file hash (B0 profile section below). The probe is bid-only; the ask value (−0.146, market data) is carried over and unused while the profile quotes bids only. The probe window starts at the simulated fill and is 1 s long; it is not the hedge arrival time (hedge arrival is about 31 ms after the fill in these runs, shown below). No haircut is applied or proposed.

The pinned TRAIN number would therefore be optimistic if SELECT is typical. Your call: keep −0.771 (as you set), or re-estimate on TRAIN plus more days; I did not use SELECT to set the number. Identity check: the probe on the live tree reproduces the stored frozen-snapshot probe for 03-01 row for row (319 fills, identical moves).

Effect of the new number on the v1.1 runs (cell b against the control cell a): the 03-11 quote moves from 141.167 to 141.165 and the one fill is 0.002 USDT better; the other days are unchanged except the count of refused quotes (below).

B0.3 Bid or ask rounded onto the touch: new switch

Rule (switch arb.join_touch_on_tick_rounding, default off). If the quote was inside the spread before tick rounding and lands exactly on the best price once rounded, it joins the back of that level's queue (the full displayed size is ahead of it) provided the edge at the rounded price passes the same gate; otherwise it is refused as before. A quote that rounds below the touch is still refused. Both sides use the rule (I built the ask side as the mirror; only the bid side is exercised in the runs). The edge check cannot actually fail after rounding: rounding a bid down (an ask up) only improves the edge, so the "refuse if the edge no longer passes" branch exists but is unreachable in practice; it is counted separately (tick_rounding_join_no_edge) so you can see if that ever changes. Queue position was checked in the engine: an order placed on a visible level gets queue ahead = the displayed size (03-09 example below: 221.6), not a fitted draw.

Profile: on in perp_perp_maker_taker_v1_2, off in v1.1. Cells on the four 2-hour slices, same code, same probe hedge move in b and c, so b against c isolates the switch (the trade logs of 03-08 and 03-11 are byte-identical between b and c; 03-09 and 03-10 differ by the joined orders).

Slicev1.1 (cell b): quotes refused on tick roundingv1_2 (cell c): signals that joined the touchOrders placed b / cFills b / cCash P&L b / c (USDT)
03-08000 / 00 / 00 / 0
03-09610 / 10 / 10 / −0.0622
03-10420 / 20 / 00 / 0
03-1123311 / 11 / 1−0.0545 / −0.0545
  • Refused versus joined are not the same unit. The refused column counts strategy evaluations (one per book event while the same quote stays refused); the joined column counts signals. After a signal is sent the maker limit blocks new ones, so 233 refusals on 03-11 do not mean 233 lost orders. The honest comparison is orders placed: v1.1 placed 1 order in the four slices, v1_2 placed 4 (the same one plus three joined ones), and 1 fill against 2.
  • 03-09: the joined order rests at 144.939 behind 221.6; it fills 1.8 s later. Its queue went from 221.6 to 0 through four depth decreases (all four flagged ambiguous, no print) before one print filled it. That fill rests on the cancel-versus-trade assumption of the proportional model, not on a print at the front. Treat it as the least certain fill in this report.
  • 03-10: two joined orders (behind 2.4 and 39.4), neither filled in its 15 s life. 03-11: the one joined signal added no order (same order count, identical trade log).
  • Four orders and two fills (one of each already existed in v1.1) cannot show whether joining the touch adds P&L; it shows only that the switch fires, behaves as specified, and leaves a no-join day byte-identical. Fees are ASSUMED; with the conservative bybit_perp_vip0 maker (+2.0 bps) the quotes, and so the counts, would differ.

Tests added: bid joins at the touch, ask joins at the touch, a quote with no room before rounding is still refused, the switch needs tick rounding, and the profile files (v1.1 off, v1_2 on). All in tests/test_quote_tick_rounding.py and tests/test_audit_decision_a.py.

Queue v2 evidence under the v1.1 fill rules (your added item)

Source: the existing probe on the four SELECT slices, run on the live tree (its P&L is not used; it quotes a bid on the tick grid every 15 s, so it is the usable sample), and the strategy slice runs. "Queue-position update" = one event (print, depth decrease or snapshot) after which a resting order's queue ahead went down. No such counter existed; I added one as observation only (MakerExecutor.queue_updates, read after the event, changes nothing; v1.1 outputs are byte-identical with it, see the gate checks). The per-fill flag is the engine's own ambiguous_trade_depth.

Step 2 diagnosis, Binance 2022-01-05 and 2022-01-13 (report only, no code changed)

The frame-atomic exact book was re-scored against every snapshot, additionally allowing a match with the rebuilt book at any point within ±50 ms and ±200 ms of the snapshot's exchange time (throwaway script, reads data only).

DaySnapshotsAs scored so far (stream position)±50 ms±200 ms
2022-01-0561,281touch price 91.519 %, price + size 73.088 %100.000 % / 100.000 %100.000 % / 100.000 %
2022-01-1360,689touch price 88.298 %, price + size 65.144 %100.000 % / 100.000 %100.000 % / 100.000 %
Result: step 2 is marked done as “consistent with snapshots”. The cause of the misses is snapshot timing. The first column reproduces the earlier numbers exactly, so the script scores the same thing. In every one of the 121,970 snapshots the touch (and its size) equals the rebuilt book at the snapshot's own exchange time; the nearest matching state is at offset 0 ms in 100 % of cases, so even the ±50 ms tolerance is not needed. The 8.5 % and 11.7 % misses at the stream position therefore come from event order: updates stamped at or before the snapshot's exchange time arrive after the snapshot in the stream, so the book is compared before they are applied. No updates look missing: zero snapshots remain unmatched at ±200 ms, so there are no mismatches to list (the 20 samples the prompt asks for in the failing case do not exist). Not measured: how late those updates arrive (the receive-time gap), and whether the same holds on Bybit or on other days. What it means for the strategy: a replay that evaluates signals in receive order sees a Binance book that lags exchange time by that gap; that is a feed-latency fact, not a rebuild error. The January snapshots may not be an independent reference: they match the deltas exactly, which fits a snapshot produced by the same collector from the same stream, so “consistent with snapshots” is evidence of internal consistency, not of agreement with the exchange.

Ledger feasibility (report only, no data re-converted)

Short answer. The ledger note's claim that March has native ids is not confirmed. I cannot show that the raw March files still exist, and I found no converter for them in the project.
  • Converted March files (checked): data/bybit/{book_events,trades,book_snapshots} for 2024-03-11 carry only exchange and local timestamps, side, price, size (and event type). No trade id, update id or sequence. The January 2022 files are the same shape (Tardis-shaped); Tardis' own trade files carry a trade id, which the conversion dropped.
  • Raw March files: not found. I searched the project tree (names containing ob500, bycsi, trdMatchID, data.binance.vision, zip and csv.gz files) and found no raw March source and no converter script; the only trace is a label in scripts/queue_v2_manifest.py and manifest_mar2024.json: "Binance: data.binance.vision futures/um bookTicker (L1 only) + trades; Bybit: bycsi linear ob500 + public trades (declared)". "Declared" means the project wrote it, the files do not say so. I did not search outside the project folders; you may have the downloads elsewhere. The two Oct 2024 archives in the project root are already in the converted layout, not raw.
  • What the sources would hold (from Bybit's live WebSocket documentation, read today; I could not check the historical files against it): order-book messages carry an update id u, a cross sequence seq and the matching-engine time cts; public trades carry a trade id i, the match time T and a cross sequence seq. The documentation says cts can be correlated with the trade's T, and that several messages can share one seq, so it is not a unique key. Whether the historical "ob500" and public-trade files hold the same fields is unknown to me; the public trade CSV files are, as far as I know, id-only (no seq).
  • Does anything link a trade to a depth update? Not in the converted files. In the raw sources the only candidates are the shared cross sequence and the matching-engine time. A trade id alone removes duplicate trades; it does not say which depth change the trade caused. Binance March has no depth, so only Bybit matters.
  • Today: the engine pairs a print with a depth decrease by a 50 ms window (dedupe_window_s), which is why SELECT shows 0.72 % of fills and 95 % of depth-driven updates marked ambiguous.
  • Proposal, nothing done: (1) you tell me where the raw March Bybit files are, or confirm the provider and I identify the files; (2) I read them (no conversion) and report which of u, seq, cts, i are present and whether a trade's seq equals the depth message's seq for the same match; (3) only if that link is real do you decide on a re-conversion into new, versioned partitions (existing files untouched), as the Phase B ledger line says. The sensitivity range comes from separate full replays at the Phase B gate; no shadow trackers are built.

Profile roles, defaults and what the v1_2 profile now carries

  • Roles (your decision, 5 Nov): v1 and legacy_v0 are reference only; v1.1 is the byte-identical control; perp_perp_maker_taker_v1_2 (new file in profiles/, needs git add -f) is v1.1 plus approved changes.
  • Default and --reference: the two March/January runners (out/queue_v2/qv2_march_run.py, qv2_run.py) now take the profile as optional and default to v1.1; legacy_v0 and v1 refuse to run without --reference, and the summary's qv2 block is labelled REFERENCE_ONLY when they do. The baseline wrapper passes --reference for its legacy and v1 jobs. Engine defaults were not touched. Not done: the many older pool scripts in out/queue_v2 and out/exec_layer_p1lite that call these runners with v1 or legacy_v0 (they would now stop and need --reference), and two scripts that hard-code v1 (scripts/perp_fault_run.py, scripts/hedge_sweep_offline.py). They are historical; tell me if you want them changed.
  • Run-defining settings moved into v1_2: maker_venues: [bybit], venue_ticks 0.001 / 0.001, block_on_toxic, max_cross_venue_skew_s 0.2, fill_gate_horizon_s 5 (inactive, min_fill_prob is 0), and hedge_move_calibration: config/hedge_move_probe_mar2024_train_0301_0303.json pinned by sha256 c267f0b2…6296fd. The profile loader refuses a changed file and refuses any run whose start date is not after every training day. The release identity records the file, hash, days and n. These values describe the March 2024 SOLUSDT set-up; January needs different ticks (Bybit 0.005) and both makers, so v1_2 as written does not run January (the loader refuses it on the training-day rule). Cell c ran from the profile alone: no environment variable supplied any of these.
  • Still supplied per run by the runner, on purpose: the data manifest path, the per-day unknown-queue fit (strictly earlier days), the run's dates and output paths.

B0: what is not proven, and decisions for you

  • Hedge move: SELECT-probe is 0.485 bps more adverse than the pinned TRAIN probe; realised n = 1. Keep −0.771, or re-estimate on more TRAIN days? (No haircut applied.)
  • Join switch: works as specified, tested, v1.1 untouched; its value is not measured (four orders, two fills). The one fill that depends on the join (03-09) rests on four ambiguous depth decreases. It is in v1_2 only.
  • Frame-atomic: the match percentage cannot show its benefit; the benefit is measured in Phase B with signals after whole messages.
  • Step 2 (Binance): marked done as “consistent with snapshots” (100 % at exchange time); the snapshots may not be an independent reference. Decide whether the receive-time lag of the late updates should be measured in Phase B.
  • Ledger native ids: deferred by the owner (6 Nov). No download and no switch. The 50 ms pairing window (dedupe_window_s) stays, and every result that depends on trade-to-depth pairing states it. Nothing was re-converted.
  • Default-profile change: the two hard-coded v1 scripts and perp_grid.py now require or pass --reference (fixed after the gate, see F1); older pool scripts that use frozen snapshots were left.
  • Speed: the counter cost 1.4 % and is now zero when observations are off; 2 to 5 % over the 378 s reference remains unexplained (see F2).

Phase A: what moved, what changed, what it proves

A2. How the old imports keep working

Each moved file in new_backtest is now three lines that make new_backtest.X the same module object as the core module (28 such files). That gives identical classes (no second copy, so no isinstance surprises), monkeypatching that still works, and every import in the tests, scripts and trading_platform_poc/minreq unchanged. Two packages (execution, execution/tactics) keep real __init__ files that re-export the same names from crypto_stat_arb.ems. No existing test was edited. Three test files were added: test_import_direction.py (you asked for it), test_moved_code_guards.py (see A4) and test_hedge_failure_catalogue.py (the catalogue check).

A3. What changed besides moving

  • new_backtest/config.py: the latency specs, presets and the two venue-name constants are no longer defined here; it imports them from simulation/latency_model.py and re-exports them (the same objects).
  • Core latency.py (two lazy imports) and time_integrity.py (one import): they now import from simulation/latency_model.py instead of new_backtest.
  • simulation/fault_presets.py: one import line, .config to .latency_model.
  • simulation/fill_engine.py: three typing-only edits. It used to import EmpiricalFillModel (a research class that must stay in new_backtest) only to annotate one argument and call record_order_event on it. A small OrderEventSink protocol now says exactly that; EmpiricalFillModel satisfies it unchanged.
  • new_backtest/profiles.py: the release identity's code hash covered new_backtest only, so it would have lost sight of the moved code. It now also hashes the moved core files. This is the one change that touches how a run is labelled; result files are unaffected.

A4. Import direction

The core no longer imports new_backtest anywhere except sdk.py, and there only inside functions (six lazy imports of the runner, kept until Phase F). tests/test_import_direction.py enforces this three ways: no core source file imports it (any depth, lazy or not), the sdk.py exception is lazy only, and importing the core package in a fresh interpreter loads no new_backtest module. I checked the test by planting a core file that imports new_backtest: it failed, then I removed the file. Because test_maker_observations.py scans src/new_backtest by path for reads of the observation log, and the producer (fill_engine.py) moved out of that folder, I added test_moved_code_guards.py with the same scan over the moved code instead of editing the original.

A5. Known temporary state: two order books and two models files

The core now has two books and two models files. This is intended for now and is not a duplicate to merge by hand.

FileWhat it isWho uses itTarget by Phase D
orderbook.pyThe core book: Decimal, applies a whole exchange message at once, validates (gap, crossed, stale), explicit states.TradingSession, OMS, risk, live feed.The book the strategy reads, in backtest, paper and live.

Hazard: both models files define a class called Side with different members (BUY/SELL against BID/ASK). Nothing imports both today. Proposed names, not applied: simulation/order_book.py to simulation/truth_book.py with the class renamed TruthBook once the strategy no longer reads it (until then the class also serves as the backtester's view book, so renaming it earlier would mislabel it); simulation/models.py to simulation/queue_types.py, with Side renamed QueueSide; the core orderbook.py and models.py keep their names. A third, research-only book also exists, simulation/queue_v2/rebuild.py (ExactBook, integer, frame-atomic). Nothing is renamed in Phase A.

A6. Speed

Reference: v1.1 on the 2024-03-11 slice, one replay alone, 378 s (Phase 0). Readings during Phase A: 376 (move 7), 398 and 391 and 390 (move 8), 391 (move 9), 420 then 389 (move 10b), 378 (move 10d). The slower readings need an honest explanation: the 398 happened while I ran a test in parallel, so it is contaminated. In the middle of the phase the machine was slower than at the start: at 12:20 I timed the pre-Phase-A tree alone and got 391 s, the same as the moved code (389 to 391). The 420 was not reproduced (389 on the rerun) and I have no explanation beyond noise. Conclusion: no measurable slowdown from the moves; the final reading is 378 s. The reading at the gate, measured alone on the finished code, is 387 s (+2.4% against the 378 s reference, and 4 s below what the pre-Phase-A tree took on the same machine at its slowest that day).

A8. What is not proven, and decisions for you

A change that only shows on another day or another path is caught at the gate, not at the move.
  • My check script for move 10b passed the test list as one argument by mistake (a shell word-splitting slip), so its tests did not run inside the script; I ran them separately right after (334 passed). The replay checks in that script were valid.
  • "Identical" means byte-identical trade log, fill events, fill statistics, model settings and every result field except wall time, paths and the release identity (the code hash changes by design).
  • The 93 failing or erroring tests are the same as at the Phase 0 gate (88 in test_ems_wiring.py and one in test_native_ems.py need a native module that is not built here; four are package and uv build environment failures). They are in out/integration/baselines/failing_tests_phase0.txt.

Phase 0 (recorded at its gate; conditions 1 to 5 done, see below)

Phase 0 conditions, all done. (1) Committed (outer 8ebd8e4) and tagged phase0-gate in both repos. (2) The exact list of 93 failing tests is saved. (3) Faulted baselines: stressed seeds 1, 2, 3 and realistic seed 2 fire hedge faults or the breaker; realistic seed 1 fires nothing and equals the clean v1.1 run, so it is kept only as a check of that path. Phase 0, 1. Preconditions and the one important fact

  • One session, no jobs: no python or pytest process was running on the tree when I started; the only jobs that ran were my own (the baseline pool and the test suite); all have finished and nothing is running now. It did not build pairing, venue selector, risk gate, breaker pause or flatten on trip. What exists is only settings and names: execution/config.py accepts pairing (maker_taker, maker_maker, taker_taker) and a maker_maker.escalate_after_s key, and backtester.py:665 raises NotImplementedError for maker_maker; there is no pairing, venue-selector or risk-gate module (the only gate is the method ExecutionLayer._risk_gate over the reservation book); circuit_breaker.py has a trip but no pause state, and the only flatten is the flatten intent role, the MARKET_BANDED tactic, and the instant legacy flatten (layer.py:348). Phase E builds the missing parts.

3. Parity tests the matrix only quoted

tests/test_order_state_parity.py and tests/test_maker_executor_engine_parity.py were run: 98 passed, 6 expected failures (xfail), 0 skipped, 17 subtests passed, in 0.4 s. The C++ queue engine did load: load_cpp_module() returns sim_engine/mm_matching_engine.cpython-312-darwin.so with no error (checked separately, because the test file leaves the C++ engine out silently if it cannot load). The six xfails are exactly the six known differences of the legacy settings: D-01 (no PostOnly reject, 2 cases), D-02 (no acknowledgement or unknown state, 3 cases), D-03 (a fill executed before a cancel but received after it is dropped, 1 case). The perp column has none. The three queue differences the matrix called "pinned" (D1 a print through our price, D2 two own orders sharing a print, D3 a late print after a depth decrease) all pass as documented differences today.

4. The scenarios (S1 to S19)

Every scenario was worked out by hand first and the numbers were written into its docstring before it ran (tests/test_integration_scenarios.py). Result of the file: 26 passed, 4 expected failures, 2 skipped (not expressible). S16 to S19 were added in the second half of Phase 0 (see 4b); all four passed on their first run as calculated. A scenario where today's code gives a different answer from the requirement is split into target (the requirement; expected to fail today) and today (pins what the code does now, so a later phase that changes it shows up on purpose).

4b. Hedge-failure case catalogue (item added after the first draft)

Every hedge-failure case in the five reports (hedge_failure_study HS, hedge_failure_deep_dive HD, lifecycle_realism LR, perp_lifecycle PL, perp_mechanics PM), read from the reports' own sections (failure modes, scenario tables, parity disagreements, conditional presets, WP6 recovery), with where each is handled today in the backtester and in the core, and which scenario or existing test pins it. 40 cases. Every test named here was checked to exist (pytest --collect-only: 0 missing); that does not prove it tests the claim. I read the loosest citations and corrected or labelled them (H14, H17, H22, H37); the rest were not individually read, so treat the table as an acceptance list to confirm at Phase C, not as proof. A case with an existing hand-driven test points to it; S16 to S19 were added for four cases; the other existing tests pin positions and counters, not fees and cash, and gain those once Phase C runs them through the core. The rest sit in S1 to S15. Phase C turns each into a scenario that runs through the core path with the old result shown beside it.

Where this list lives. From now on the table above is the source: I built it from a data file and a generator that exist only in this session's scratch area, not in the project. Phase C needs the 40-case list, so I ask where it should live (proposal: a data file next to tests/test_integration_scenarios.py, referenced from the Phase C scenarios).

Summary of the mapping. Same rule in both stacks: H07, H11, H12, H15, H17, H18, H19, H21, H24, H28, H29, H30, H31, H32, H34 (and H13, H37 in intent). Conflicts, where the requirement or the core rule wins: H08 (the backtester unwinds after the timeout without a query; OMS03), H09 (ghost over-hedge; OMS03 and Demonstration 1), H22 (breaker trips at 5 s net exposure in the backtester, the core escalates a residual breach at 30 s), plus assumed numbers that differ: H02 and H20 (unwind band 15 vs 10 bps), H16 (alarm vs kill switch). In the backtester only, to port in Phase C or E: H03 and H04 (retry before unwind), H23 (breaker: six triggers, pause vs trip), H25 (flatten on trip), H27 (reject classification, venue_semantics_perp), H39 (hedge venue choice), the entry guard H14 (knows the gateway state from the breaker's monitor). Gap in the core (EMS01): H10, a request queued for rate limits is sent against the book captured when it was queued. Stay in simulation/ (injection or legacy oracle): H05, H06 (the injection side), H26, H40. Compared in the Phase B accounting shadow: H35, H38.

What the scenarios found

  • Scenario 5 is the open ghost defect, now pinned, with both halves of Demonstration 1. With a gateway outage the backtester writes the hedge off after 2 s, unwinds the maker fill, and the original hedge then lands at reconnect: the account ends short 1.2 that nobody asked for. The requirement (OMS03, Demonstration 1) says query before replacing and never understate the reserve. The reserve half works today only with the profile's mandatory switches (count_pending_exposure, count_unknown_exposure): on, the 1.2 binance sells are held from 6.0 s until the ghost lands; off (the legacy setting) the reserve is empty while 1.2 is live. The replacement half fails. Phase E must turn the target test green and update the today tests.
  • Scenario 13: the maker engine's answer depends on the order of two events. With two of our orders at one level, MakerExecutor gives a different result depending on whether the print or the matching depth update arrives first (print first: the second order gets nothing; depth first: both fill). I checked it under two configurations: the parity test's (trades only, all_ahead) and the maker block of perp_perp_maker_taker_v1_1 (proportional, trade_confirm, print-against-depth netting): same two answers in both. The core queue engine is order-independent and uses one budget. The under-fill half is the already-pinned difference D2; what is new is that it also depends on event order. It cannot occur in v1.1 runs (maker_max_live_orders is 1 in every profile, so two own orders never share a level); it becomes reachable when more than one live order is allowed, which is a Phase D/E question.
  • Scenario 3b confirms C9 with a concrete case. In the legacy setting (fill_evidence: off), the level we rest on going 3 to 0 to 2 to 0 with no print at all fills 0.3 and the hedge follows. The v1.1 setting does not. My first draft of this scenario passed in both modes and proved nothing; the depth-only sequence above is the one that shows it.
  • Scenario 11: the liquidation fill pays the venue taker fee and the liquidation fee. That is by design in the backtester (a code comment says so). My hand calculation assumed the liquidation fee replaces the taker fee, so it was wrong by 0.088. Whether a real venue charges both is a Phase B question (matrix A10).
  • Scenario 15 is partly covered already. The brief said this window has no test. A close one exists (test_restart_resilience.py: an adapter raises after the intent is durable), but it raises an ordinary exception, so the OMS writes an "ambiguous dispatch" record. My scenario raises a kill the OMS cannot catch, so nothing is recorded. After the restart the OMS re-arms a report deadline for both children, queries the venue for each, sends nothing, keeps the 1,200.60 reservation, and does not trip the kill switch. I expected a gap here and there is none. What cannot be tested yet is the last step: a replacement after the venue says "unknown", which needs the Phase C deferred send path. Open for Phase C: the BYBIT child was never sent and re-executing does not send it, so the package stays half-dispatched (BINANCE unresolved, BYBIT never sent) until the queries time out; nothing decides what happens to the second leg.

Corrections to my own hand calculations on the first run

On the first run 5 of the 24 tests did not match what I had written down (the other 19 passed as written). Four of those were my errors and were corrected in the file with a "FIRST-RUN CORRECTION" note (the fifth was a placeholder for scenario 13's observation): scenario 10 (my driver skipped the breaker handler run() calls after every event, so the cancel counter stayed 0; the expectation stands), scenario 11 (the fee point above), scenario 12 (a plan with one filled child is PARTIAL, not DISPATCHED), scenario 15 (no gap, above). Scenario 3b was also rebuilt after its first form proved nothing.

Notes on how the scenarios were built

  • Reuse. The scripted Harness of test_maker_order_lifetime.py, the margin and hedge-failure helpers, the PostOnly harness, ExecutorAdapter of the maker parity test and the restart-test helpers were reused; no generator was written. synthetic_days.py and selftest.py were not used: they produce random or real-derived event streams whose exact orders and fills cannot be worked out by hand, which is what these scenarios need. They are the right tools for the Phase D parity runs on synthetic data.
  • Latency. The Harness uses the colocated_both preset with pinned entry and cancel delays. That preset is a sensitivity-only upper bound for results; here it only fixes the arithmetic. No scenario produces a P&L figure.
  • Claims register. I read its sections "wrong or stale" and "conflicts between reports". Nothing in them changes a scenario. Two touch Phase 0: the breaker "had never run until 1 Nov" (scenario 10 now runs it through the backtester), and every P&L figure in four earlier reports is a colocated_both upper bound (so none of them is a baseline here).
  • compare_baselines.py will be used by every later phase, so it is logic and not a run wrapper. It sits in out/integration/baselines/ for now; I propose moving it to scripts/ when you approve a new file there.

Since the first draft: re-read, fees, naming

  • Re-read. I re-read the prompt and the plan in full after both changed (prompt 12,393 bytes at 04:23, plan 14,870 bytes at 04:17). Their Phase 0 text is unchanged; the additions are the fee decision, the rule-versus-simulation split in Phase C, and the instruction to carry the hedge-failure cases through the core. No finished result is invalidated, so no baseline, determinism run or test run was repeated: the baselines use v1.1's existing fee presets (bybit_perp_mm maker -0.5, taker 5.5; binance_perp_vip0 2.0/5.0), which is the owner's headline, and the changes to the fee scenario, the core config and the hedging port all belong to Phases B and C.
  • Fees, as the baselines used them: ASSUMED, not verified. The rebate size, the Bybit taker rate and the Binance tier are unconfirmed until you give the account's own SOLUSDT figures. bybit_perp_vip0 (+2.0 maker) is the conservative scenario and has no baseline here; the core's config/perp_research.json still says +2.0 and is a Phase B change.
  • Naming. "Phase 0 / Gate 0" in canonical_execution_layer_simplified.html is the execution-layer work already done in new_backtest/execution. This report's Phase 0 is baselines and scenarios. Nothing of the execution layer was rebuilt.
  • Hedging is ported, not reinvented (Phase C). The catalogue above is the inventory of what already exists: in the backtester the fill-triggered hedge, retry, unwind, ghost, UNKNOWN, alarm and breaker code; in the core the residual, unwind, alarm, deadline and recovery paths. The gaps it shows for Phase C are listed in the next paragraph.

5. What is not proven

  • Synthetic scripts prove wiring only; no profit figure comes from them. The baselines are code-identity references on three data sets, not results.
  • The core cannot run scenarios 1 to 4, 6 today (no fill-triggered hedge, no queue fills on the platform path): the comparison with the core is by reading, not by running, until Phase C and D.
  • Replay speed is not measured by these baselines against anything yet; the wall times above are the reference for Phase A's "note the replay speed".
  • Baselines cover the clean case only. Faulted replays (the realistic and stressed presets) have no baseline; if you want them as identity checks they add hours.
  • The Binance side of March is best bid/ask only; no scenario or baseline says anything about Binance depth in March.

6. Test count

Full suite on the live tree at the end of Phase 0 (PYTHONPATH=src python3.12 -m pytest from the platform root, with collection errors continued): 92 failed, 2601 passed, 4 skipped, 10 xfailed, 150 warnings, 1 error, 103 subtests passed in 1154.21s (0:19:14). The integration-port note of 2 Nov recorded 1,409 passing on this branch; the difference is explained in the next paragraph.

Failures, as they stand (93 in total, all under tests/platform_kit/, none in the core or backtester tests):

  • 89 are ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'hft_platform_kit._ems': 88 in test_ems_wiring.py and the collection error of test_native_ems.py. The native extension is not in the tree (src/hft_platform_kit holds only cli.py, protocols.py, sdk.py); it has never been built on this machine.
  • 4 are environment: two report the distribution version as 0.0.0+local (package not installed), test_release_artifacts_are_clean_and_native fails because uv build returns non-zero, and test_opportunity_demo fails because its subprocess python3.12 -m trading_platform_poc.cli exits non-zero. I re-ran the last two alone, with nothing else running, and they still fail, so they are not load effects.
  • No test marked timing or concurrency failed. I did not run the suite at the restore tag, so "these failed before Phase 0" is an inference from the causes (a module that does not exist, an uninstalled package), not a measurement.

The 1,409 of 2 Nov is not comparable. I do not know which directories that count included. Today's run is the whole tests/ tree (143 files, including tests/platform_kit, the integration-port tests, the queue v2 tests and my new file): 2,601 passed, 4 skipped, 10 xfailed, 103 subtests passed. My new file is in those numbers as it stood when the suite started (19 passed, 2 skipped, 3 xfailed); I then added checks and four more scenarios (S16 to S19) and ran its final form alone: 26 passed, 2 skipped, 4 xfailed. The suite took 19 min 14 s with three replays running at the same time.
